# Pip v4: design-review packet #2

A general-purpose home robot: omni base, a lift on a mast, two 7-DOF arms (6 joints + gripper), a head with a face screen. 3D-printed (PETG + ASA near motors) on a Bambu P2S (256 mm bed) around off-the-shelf actuators: SteadyWin GIM6010-8 (24 V), Damiao DM-J4310-2EC V1.1, Feetech STS3215 12 V. Everything is generated from rnd/params.py: build123d CAD, MuJoCo twins, FEA (scikit-fem, P1 and P2 tets), Monte Carlo checks. Numbers marked ESTIMATE are unmeasured.

**Context:** review #1 (another model, 2026-11-26) found 18 issues; docs/review/triage_2026-11-26.md lists them and RND_PLAN §4 shows what loop 40 did about each. The owner is about to buy the **M1 one-arm bench kit** (docs/M1_BENCH_ARM.md) and wants an independent check first.


=== FILE: docs/M1_READINESS.md ===
# M1 bench arm: purchase readiness (generated by `python -m rnd.m1_readiness`)

**Kit:** $1,290 + 15% contingency = **$1,484** ([M1_BENCH_ARM.md](M1_BENCH_ARM.md)).

**Verdict: buy the motors and electronics now; buy the D13 drive parts and belts with them, checking the listed dimensions.** Nothing found in loop 40 changes the motor choice, and the design changes it did force (80T roll pulley, D13 shafts, D16 spindle, D15 springs, die spring, regen clamp, second CAN channel, ASA) are all in the kit list. What remains uncertain is printed-part behaviour (stiffness, coupling slip and creep, clock-spring life), and that is what M1 and the coupons are for.

| What you're buying / relying on | Evidence (checks in this repo) | Confidence | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Motors: 4 × GIM6010-8, 1 × DM-J4310, 2 × STS3215 | whole-workspace margin ≥ 1.53× rated (p95, 1 kg), ≥ 2.58× peak; hottest winding 69 °C on a non-stop task day | high (90 %) | The only buy that is expensive to undo. Every joint passes with the loop-40 masses (heavier shoulder and housing included). |
| Gear ratios (belts, pulleys) | pitch 3.0, roll 4.44 (80T), yaw 2.0, elbow 2.0; belt pull ≥ 1.5× working rating | high (85 %) | Pulleys are cheap to change later; the motors don't depend on them. |
| D13 drive parts (Ø12 shafts, couplings, aluminium pulleys, 6801s) | R14: 6/6 shafts assemble; torque pins 21/21 | medium-high (75 %) | Dimensions are ESTIMATE until the parts are in hand (check bore 12 mm on the pulleys). |
| Bearings (61808, 6708, 6801, 61804, U604) | counted from the CAD (loop 40) | high (90 %) | Cheap; a 10-pack of each covers spares. |
| Twist-lock couplings (D15 hardware) | slip below rated 0.00%; below peak 13% after 10-year creep | medium (70 %) | Coupon C1 measures the real slip, twist and repeatability; springs come as an assortment. |
| Balancer (die spring, 2:1 tackle) | fits the 135 mm cage at 35 % of L0 (limit 40 %) | medium (70 %) | Pick the catalogue rate ≥ 12 N/mm; K adjusts with the pulley slot. |
| Bench electronics (24 V PSU, regen clamp, e-stop, 2 × USB-CAN) | A5 + A14 (electrical.md) | high (85 %) | The regen clamp is the one line that must not be skipped: the PSU can't absorb back-driven energy and the GIM is rated 28 V. |
| Cable routing (ribbon stock, slim USB, CSI camera) | 6/6 joints cross their full range; yaw ±150° | medium (65 %) | Clock-spring life is untested: add coupon C11 (10k cycles). |
| Printed structure (not bought, but it decides whether M1 meets its accuracy goal) | hand deflection 12 mm raw, 2.4 mm after a gravity model (P2 FEA chain) | medium (60 %) | Loop 40 found the shells far softer with P2 elements and stiffened them; the bench arm measures it. Doesn't change what to buy. |
| Dexterity | top-down 34%, 45° 70%, side 66%, across the body 54% | high (85 %) | Yaw ±150° is back. |
| Spacing / swept clearances | static hard violations 0; swept roll/yaw 0; module clashes 0 | high (85 %) |  |

## What could still make the purchase wrong

- **Wrong motor variant delivered** (48 V GIM listing, J4310 V1.2 vs V1.1, 7.4 V STS3215): check the listing against M1_BENCH_ARM.md before paying.
- **The GIM6010 CAN protocol** couldn't be read here (the manual is blocked). If it had no impedance/torque mode, control would fall back to position mode with torque limits: workable for the bench, weaker for learning policies. Ask the seller for the protocol document.
- **DM-J4310 output-bearing moment rating** (≈ 0.9 N·m dynamic at the wrist pitch): ask the seller.
- **Printed parts:** if C1 shows KX slip under rated torque, or M1 shows hand sag far above the chain estimate, the fix is in printed parts and fasteners (stiffer shells, PETG-CF / ASA, bolted joints), not in the motors.

## Before the motors arrive

1. Owner's own reviews (Fable, Astra): packets in docs/review/ (`python -m rnd.review_packet`).
2. Print coupons C1–C9 as soon as the P2S arrives (C1 is now the D15 screwed socket).
3. Bench prep: build the regen clamp and e-stop board first; first power-up of each motor current-limited at 1 A.

# Milestone M1: one-arm bench kit (generated by `python -m rnd.m1_kit`)

**≈ $1,290 + 15% contingency = $1,484** (loop 40 refresh for the buy decision). 6 lines are priced from retailer snapshots, 22 are ESTIMATE. Everything except the bench supply is reused in the full robot.

**What it proves on real hardware:**
- joint strength, belt behaviour and the D13 stages
- the D15 KX sockets under real loads (slip, twist, re-seat repeatability)
- the shoulder balancer (A9)
- hand accuracy with the joint encoders (target ≤ 1 mm), and the compliance chain (rnd/stiffness.py)
- the fin-ray grasp and the D16 wrist-roll spindle
- motor and can temperatures (A6), CAN timing (SW1, A14), e-stop time (SW2)

## What changed since the loop-39 list

- **Bearings:** 8 × 61804 + 4 × 6805 → 8 × 6801 (D13 stage shafts) + 2 × 61804 (D16 wrist-roll spindle) + 2 × 6708 (elbow cassette) + 2 × U604 (balancer).
- **D13 standard parts (new):** Ø12 steel shafts, 4 aluminium flange couplings, 4 aluminium HTD pulleys (were printed small hubs).
- **Roll belt:** 475 mm (80T roll pulley, loop 40); the others are unchanged.
- **KX hardware (D15):** coil springs (6 per socket) in place of a wave-spring stack, which never fitted; 5 sockets (the wrist roll lost its KX44, D16).
- **Balancer (A9):** a die spring through a 2:1 tackle in place of a 3 N/mm compression spring, which could not fit its cage.
- **Electronics:** regen clamp (A5, mandatory on a bench supply), 2 USB-CAN channels (A14), elbow-node buck + TTL (D14), 2-channel e-stop + watchdog.
- **Filament:** + 1 kg ASA for the motor cans and motor-side couplings (A6).
- **+15 % contingency** for price drift, shipping and tax (A16).

## Check with the seller before paying

- GIM6010-8: the **24 V SDC104** driver version (some Amazon listings are 48 V with other torque figures); ask for the installation drawing (bolt circles are ESTIMATE; coupon C9 reads them).
- DM-J4310-2EC: **V1.1** (V1.2 listings mix in J4310P figures). Ask the seller for the output bearing's allowable radial load and moment (wrist pitch carries ≈ 0.9 N·m bending, dynamic).
- STS3215: the **12 V C018** (30 kg·cm) version, not the 7.4 V one.
- Aluminium HTD 5M pulleys: 15 mm belt width, **12 mm bore** (many default to 8 mm; bore-to-order is common), with two set screws.
- Belts: closed HTD **5M**, 15 mm wide, the four lengths above (± 5 mm is taken up by the eccentric bushings: ±1 mm centre ≈ ±2 mm length).

**Order of work:** the coupon round C1 ([COUPONS.md](COUPONS.md)) can run in parallel once the P2S arrives; the actuators, bearings and electronics don't depend on any printed coupon result. Buying is the owner's decision.

=== FILE: docs/RND_PLAN.md ===
# Pip v4: hardware R&D, operating model and plan

**Scope:** the robot's **physical design** (mechanical, electrical, industrial design, simulation, cost). The AI stack comes later.

**Concept:** [V4_CONCEPT.md](V4_CONCEPT.md). **Live numbers:** [rnd/SCORECARD.md](rnd/SCORECARD.md). **History:** [RND_LOG.md](RND_LOG.md).

**Owner's brief (2026-11-25):** Claude runs the development. Iterate over long periods, innovate, change course when the evidence says so, and don't stop at the first "done". More compute → a better robot.

---

## 1. Operating model: a continuous engineering loop

Each scheduled run (and any interactive session) does one **loop**:

1. **Sync:** `git pull`; read the scorecard, the work queue (§4), and the last few log entries.
2. **Critique.** Ask what the single highest-leverage thing is right now. Candidates:
   - the **weakest scorecard metric**
   - the **biggest unretired risk**
   - the **next thing a build needs**
   - a **better idea** that could beat the current design (look for precedent first)
3. **Do the work** with real tools:
   - build123d CAD
   - MuJoCo twin and analyses (`python -m rnd.run_all`)
   - Blender renders
   - web research with sources
   - parallel subagents for independent research, where useful
4. **Measure:** regenerate the reports and update the scorecard. **A change that makes the scorecard worse is reverted unless the log explains why it's worth it.**
5. **Decide:** update the decision table (§3). Decisions **can be reversed**; record why. Edit the queue freely: add, split, reorder or delete.
6. **Record:** commit and push to `claude/robot-dog-p2s-printer-j4esie`, and add a log entry: what, numbers before → after, files, open questions.
7. **Tell the owner:** a short summary every run. A **digest** (renders + scorecard delta + decisions taken) every ~4 runs, or after any major change. Digests **don't block**; work continues.

**External review budget (owner, 2026-11-26):** Gemini 4 Neon (`rnd/external_review.py`) costs ≈ $1.67 per run. Use it **at most once per day**, and only after a significant batch of fixes. The script refuses a second run within 24 h.

**Cadence (owner, 2026-11-26):** runs every 30 minutes (two hourly scheduled tasks at :10 and :40, since one task can't run more often than hourly).

**Two tracks (owner, 2026-11-26):** hardware (this plan) and **software** ([SOFTWARE_PLAN.md](SOFTWARE_PLAN.md), starting with the bench arm). Runs **alternate**: the :10 run does hardware, the :40 run does software, unless one track is blocked.

**Efficiency rules (owner, 2026-11-26: "make more progress per run"):**
1. **One concrete deliverable per run**, picked in under 2 minutes from the queue top. No re-deliberating settled decisions.
2. **Incremental checks only.** Run the checks that cover what changed:
   - CAD of the arm → integration, spacing, assembly_seq
   - masses → spacing/workspace via `run_all --quick`

   The slow full set (FEA, dynamic sim, print plan, Monte Carlos, full `run_all`) runs only when its inputs changed, and at most every 4th run.
3. **Long jobs go to the background** while the next step proceeds. Run independent checks in parallel processes.
4. **Commit early** (at least once per run), with a short log entry (5 lines max) and a short owner summary (3 lines max).
5. **Don't chase the last 1 %:** stop when the deliverable passes its check. Queue polish separately.

**The loop never "finishes".** When the queue is empty, the next run starts with a fresh critique of the whole design and writes a new queue: a stronger design, cheaper parts, easier assembly, better looks.

**Guardrails:**
- No purchases, PRs or AI-stack work.
- Every part ≤ 256 mm (P2S).
- Every actuator in a joint cartridge.
- Numbers come with sources or are marked `ESTIMATE`.
- Keep `rnd/params.py` the single source of truth.
- Only the owner decides to **buy hardware**. Milestones below end with a buy/no-buy question.

## 2. Requirements (measurable)

| ID | Requirement | Target | Hard limit |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Reach | Floor to 1.40 m: floor, dishwasher, table 0.75 m, counter 0.90 m, shelves 1.2 and 1.4 m, out to 0.60 m from the robot centre | Floor to 1.25 m |
| R2 | Payload per arm | 1.0 kg at 45 cm; 2.5 kg two-handed near the body | 0.6 kg at reach |
| R3 | Carry up stairs | ≤ 2 trips, pieces ≤ 10 kg with handles, split/join ≤ 30 s without tools, "carry mode" | Pieces ≤ 13 kg |
| R4 | Carpet | Low and medium pile, rugs, 15 mm thresholds, accurate position estimate | Low pile + 10 mm |
| R5 | Footprint | Base ≤ 50 cm; fits 70 cm doors with arms folded | ≤ 60 cm |
| R6 | Speed | 0.8 m/s, smooth holonomic motion | 0.5 m/s |
| R7 | Runtime | 2 h light duty | 1 h |
| R8 | Cost (excluding Pi) | ≤ $4,000 (was $3,300; raised for the D9 sensor pack, owner 2026-11-25) | $5,000 |
| R9 | Reusable actuators | All motors in joint cartridges | – |
| R10 | Assembly | **No hard target (owner, 2026-11-26), but reducing it is a priority.** Track first-build hands-on hours every run; reconfiguration ≤ 1 h | – |
| R11 | Looks and motion | Reads as a robot: clean shells, expressed joints, light rings, expressive head, smooth motion | – |
| R12 | Safety | Torque-limited joints, e-stop, protected 24 V, stable (tip margin ≥ 50 mm under worst static load) | – |
| R13 | Printability | Parts ≤ 256 mm, support-free where possible | – |
| R14 | **Assembly simulation** (owner, 2026-11-26) | Every part has a simulated assembly sequence: (a) each bearing slides onto its journal from a free end; (b) each part reaches its installed position along a straight insertion path without colliding with parts already installed; (c) every screw has tool access (Ø12 mm clear along its axis for ≥ 50 mm for a hex key or driver); (d) every joint cartridge can be swapped with the arm assembled. Checked by `rnd/assembly_seq.py` | 100 % of parts | all drive shafts and cartridges |
| R15 | **Spacing** (owner, 2026-11-26) | Minimum clearance between parts that are not fused or bolted together: ≥ 1.0 mm static; running clearances set by a bearing, shim or guide ≥ 0.3 mm (loop 36 refinement); ≥ 2.0 mm between other parts in relative motion over their full joint range (swept); belts ≥ 3 mm from any static part; cable bend radius ≥ 10 × OD; no belt/pulley nip reachable by a 12 mm finger probe. Checked by `rnd/spacing.py` | as stated | ≥ 0.5 mm static, ≥ 1.0 mm moving |

## 3. Decisions (living; reversals logged)

| # | Decision | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | **Carry split: upper core / base / battery / 2 arms.** Each arm detaches at the shoulder-pitch output with a **KX-V V-band clamp** (printed 3" sanitary flanges, 3 dowels, stock quick-release clamp; loop 7: the twist collar can't carry the arm's bending) plus a blind-mate plug. Core 6.9 kg, arm 3.4 kg each, base 5.8 kg, battery 1.5 kg. Two trips: core + base, then battery + arms. *Changed* (loop 4): CAD masses put the one-piece upper at 11.9 kg (limit 10) | Changed 2026-11-25 (loop 4) | [budget](rnd/budget.md) |
| D2 | **Base: 4 × 6" omni, one wheel pointing forward, wheels at R 0.21 m, Ø500 shell.** *Reversed from 3-wheel kiwi:* kiwi tip margin was ~35–46 mm vs 66–82 mm | Changed 2026-11-25 | [twin](rnd/twin.md) |
| D2b | Cross thresholds at a **45° heading and ≤ 0.15 m/s** (loop 12 roller-level sim: 0° fails at 15 mm; 0.30 m/s reaches the GIM peak by impact, 0.15 m/s peaks ~6 N·m, 1.8×). Cap base acceleration at 1.0 m/s² with arms parked and 0.5 m/s² while manipulating. Position from optical flow + IMU + lidar | Updated (loop 12) | [threshold](rnd/threshold.md) |
| D3 | **Joint cartridges carry torque only (KX v1).** The cartridge output has a KX male face: 3 hardened balls on 3 pairs of steel dowel pins, locked by a spring-preloaded breech-lock collar (35° twist, thumb tabs, self-locking 6° ramp). It locks into a **hub that turns in the bone's own bearing pair**, and the housing is held by a **steel torque pin in a radial slot**. Sizes: KX74 (GIM6010-8), **KX56 (DM-J4310; added loop 4, saves ~45 g per socket and shrinks the wrist collar from Ø98 to Ø78)** and KX44 (STS3215). The seat doubles as an overload clutch (slip ≥ 1.16× actuator peak). Buses: 24 V CAN 1 Mbps / 12 V TTL. *Changed* from the same printed male face on both sides of every cartridge: that put arm bending (up to 12 N·m) and belt pull through printed V-grooves at 3–4× PETG yield. | Changed 2026-11-25 (loop 2) | [kx](rnd/kx.md) |
| D4 | **Arm: 0.30 + 0.28 + 0.13 m, wrist ±120°** (loop 1: ±140° folded the hand into the forearm; ±120° gives more collision-free reach). **CAD requirement:** forearm bone ends ≥ 60 mm before the wrist axis. Shoulder pitch GIM × 2.5 belt + **adjustable balancer K = 4 N·m** (τ = K·sin pitch; zero-free-length cable + 2.7 N/mm caged compression spring, pulley A on a 45–65 mm slot → K 3.6–5.2 N·m, [balancer](rnd/balancer.md)), roll GIM × **3.5**, upper-arm yaw **GIM × 2 belt** (loop 5: DM-J4310 direct was under-rated over the workspace), elbow GIM × 2, wrist pitch **DM-J4310**, wrist roll and gripper STS3215. Joints are sized on a **whole-workspace check** ([spring](rnd/spring.md)), not only the straight-arm case. *Changed* from 0.25 + 0.23, ±110°, wrist STS (reach coverage 31 % → 100 %; STS wrist was under-rated) | Changed 2026-11-25 | [arm sizing](rnd/arm_sizing.md), [twin](rnd/twin.md) |
| D4b | **Counter grasps use top-down or 45° approach, whichever is collision-free** (top-down alone reaches 62 % of counter cells, combined 100 %). Telescoping spine deferred: it would cost ~0.6 kg on a tight carry budget. Revisit if real tests show the need. | New (loop 1b) | [twin](rnd/twin.md) |
| D4d | **Bones: 1.2 mm printed shells + carbon tubes** for the upper-arm neck (Ø40×1) and the forearm link (Ø30×1), bonded into printed sockets. Stiffness, not strength, limits printed bones: the hand sagged 4.1 mm (printed only) → **1.6 mm** with CF. Precedent: Hello Robot Stretch, Kinova carbon links | New (loop 9) | [bones](rnd/bones.md) |
| D5 | **Lift: shoulder height 0.35–1.05 m. Standard GIM6010-8 cartridge on a 20T HTD 5M belt along the spine, with a spring-applied, solenoid-released ratchet pawl** (fail-safe, 0 W holding, safe carry mode). 2.1× torque margin, 3.5 s full travel, 0.55 kg. *Changed* from a NEMA23 + brake (1.25 kg, another motor type); constant-force springs rejected (they shoot up when the arms are removed; fatigue) | Changed 2026-11-25 (loop 8) | [lift](rnd/lift.md) |
| D8 | **Head fixed on top of the mast** (owner's suggestion). The torso and arms ride the lift below it. Overall height is a constant **1.29 m**. The head camera always has the high vantage (tilts 60° down for floor work, level/up for shelves; precedent: Hello Robot Stretch). The bare mast no longer pokes through the head, and 0.6 kg leaves the lift's moving mass. Reach 98 %, bimanual 4/4 and tip margin unchanged | New 2026-11-25 | [head views](rnd/img/head_camera_views.png), [render](rnd/img/robot_head_on_mast.png) |
| D9 | **Sensors favour accuracy and dexterity over cost** (owner, 2026-11-25). Sensor pack v2: joint-side absolute encoders on the 8 arm belt joints + lift (hand error 4.0 → 0.9 mm); global-shutter wrist cameras; magnetic-elastomer fingertip tactile pads (AnySkin/ReSkin principle); palm ToF; base cliff ToF; optical flow kept; 3 RP2040 CAN sensor nodes. +$276 | New (loop 15) | [accuracy](rnd/accuracy.md), [BOM](rnd/BOM_v4.md) |
| D10 | **Compute:** the owner's Pi 5 runs I/O, real-time supervision, IK and state estimation (≈ 73 % of 4 cores, ESTIMATE). The torso **reserves a bay + 19 V 3 A branch + Ethernet for a Jetson-class AI computer**, bought in the AI phase (~$249) | New (loop 17) | [compute](rnd/compute.md) |
| D11 | **Head face screen + touch panel** (owner, 2026-11-26). A bar-format LCD (~7–8", to fit the 196 × 66 mm visor; panel dimensions to verify) shows the face (the eyes are drawn on it) and status, and gives touch controls: pause/resume, home/park, joint jog, teach mode, task pick, and a STOP command. It runs on **its own ESP32-S3-class node on the CAN bus**, not on the Pi, so it still works if the Pi hangs, and the cable to the fixed head is power + one CAN pair. **The hard-wired torso e-stop stays the safety device**; the screen's STOP is a command beside it, not instead of it. ~$50–90, ~+150 g at the mast top, ~2–3 W | Adopted; design in queue item 5 | owner Q&A 2026-11-26 |
| D12 | **Cable routing: a clock-spring spiral at every joint; slim bundle.** (Loop 40: the yaw cavity is real CAD and yaw is back to ±150°, D13b.) Torsion through the short shaft bores can't absorb the ranges (cable ~±360°/m). The wrist camera becomes MIPI-CSI on an FFC through the wrist, with a CSI→USB bridge on the forearm and a slim 4-core USB (≤ Ø2.2) onward. The stiffest cable is then Ø2.2 mm (bend r 22 mm). | Adopted (loop 32); yaw cavity open | [harness.md](rnd/harness.md) |
| D13 | **Belt stage v2 (assemblability, review #1 finding 1).** Small-pulley stage: a **steel Ø12 shaft** in 2 × 6801 (12×21×5) inside thicker eccentric bushings; an **off-the-shelf aluminium HTD 5M pulley** (20T; **18T on the roll stage**, fixing the 20/18 mismatch) with set screws on a flat; a **clamp flange coupling** bolted to the printed KX74 hub on a 4 × M4 circle (≈ 5 MPa on the PETG at 12.7 N·m). The bearings slide on from the free end; the shaft drops through the walls. Big-pulley stages (pitch 60T, roll 72T, yaw 40T, elbow 40T): the printed pulley becomes a **separate part bolted to a shaft flange** (≥ 4 × M4), inserted between the walls before the shaft. Every shaft→link joint gets a key or a bolted flange. ≈ +$68 net for 9 small stages (6801s replace 61804s). | Adopted (loop 35); CAD in queue A1 | [assembly_seq.md](rnd/assembly_seq.md), review #1 |
| D14 | **Only 24 V + CAN + USB cross the shoulder.** The elbow sensor node makes 12 V (buck) and drives the TTL servo bus for the wrist roll and gripper, so the clock-spring ribbon through pitch, roll and yaw is 7.8 mm wide (2 × 20 AWG, 2 CAN pairs, slim USB). | Adopted (loop 40) | [harness.md](rnd/harness.md), [electrical.md](rnd/electrical.md) |
| D15 | **KX socket as built:** screwed retainer (3 × M3), **6 coil springs Ø4 in pockets** in a 5.5 mm hub step (installed 5 mm, 3 mm compression), pins 1 mm prouder and balls 1 mm deeper in the male face (captured past the equator + retaining compound). Replaces the print-in-place socket (its 1 mm annulus held no real spring; the pause order was wrong). KX Monte Carlo: below-peak slips 26 % → 13 %. | Adopted (loop 40; review finding 2) | [kx_mc.md](rnd/kx_mc.md), COUPONS C1/C2 |
| D16 | **Wrist roll on a bearing spindle:** the gripper bolts to a spindle in 2 × 61804 under the palm; the STS3215 drives it through a two-dog coupling (torque only). The KX44 seat would lift off at ≈ 0.6 N·m against ≈ 1.3 N·m dynamic with 1 kg. l_hand 0.15 → 0.156 m. Tools bolt to the spindle flange. The wrist-pitch KX56 stays (its bending lever is only the ≈ 40 mm hub offset). | Adopted (loop 40; review finding 5) | rnd/parts/hand.py |
| D17 | **Wheels bolt to the GIM output** (no KX on the wheels: slip risk under threshold torques, review finding 12). A 3 mm aluminium ring ties the four deck quarters. | Adopted (loop 40) | [BOM](rnd/BOM_v4.md) |
| D18 | **Anti-tip casters:** four spring-loaded ball-transfer units at the diagonals under the skirt (5 N preload, 5 mm stroke) turn the x-drive diamond (148 mm to the edge between wheels) into an octagon. Push-over force at 1 m between wheels 35 → 45 N. Plus planner rules for loads over 1 kg. | Adopted (loop 40; review finding 11) | [tip.md](rnd/tip.md) |
| D13b | **Loop 40 completes D13 on the arm:** roll (18T:80T, ratio 4.44, belt 475 mm) and yaw on the D13 stage; every big pulley (pitch 60T, roll 80T, yaw 40T, elbow 40T) is split from its Ø40 tube and keyed by a double-D or a hex; yaw ±150° with real clock-spring cassettes; yaw block closed into a box; elbow housing stiffened (P2 FEA). Lift still on its pulley-on-KX layout (queue). | Done (loop 40) | [assembly_seq.md](rnd/assembly_seq.md), [swept.md](rnd/swept.md), [stiffness.md](rnd/stiffness.md) |
| D6 | Toolchain: build123d CAD, MuJoCo twin generated from `rnd/params.py`, **arm collision geometry from CAD hulls** (`rnd/parts/collision.py`, loop 6), Blender renders | Holding | `rnd/` |
| D4c | **Belt stages: HTD 5M, 15 mm, 20T small pulley** (pitch 20:50, roll 20:60, elbow 20:40), printed pulleys, the small pulley on its own bearing hub. **Elbow motor stays at the elbow:** a remote motor on a long belt (Blue-style) was evaluated and rejected (–0.5 N·m at the shoulder, +0.24 kg) | New (loop 3) | [belts](rnd/belts.md) |
| D7 | **Power bus 24 V (6S Li-ion)**, replacing 48 V | Holding | [T01](rnd/T01_specs.md) |

## 4. Work queue (ordered; edit freely)

**Milestone M2 reached (2026-11-25 build-out session):**
- every module in CAD; full assembly with 0 cross-module clashes; 59/59 printed pieces fit the P2S
- dynamic twin v1 completes a drive + floor pick + table place within real actuator limits (8/8 robust)
- electrical plan, itemised BOM ($3,220), print plan (10.4 kg, ~255 h P2S) and build guide (~25 h hands-on)
- see [BUILD_v4.md](BUILD_v4.md)

**Loop 40 (owner: "work through the whole backlog before I buy the arm", 2026-11-26).** Closed in this push, with the check that closes each:

| Item | Result | Check |
|---|---|---|
| Arm dexterity (top priority) | yaw back to ±150° with a real clock-spring cassette; side grasps across the body 2 % → 57 % | [dexterity.md](rnd/dexterity.md) (new R16 row) |

| A5 e-stop | category-0 hardware stop, pre-charge, regen clamp (mandatory on the bench supply), servos behind the contactor, watchdog | [electrical.md](rnd/electrical.md) |
| A6 PETG thermal | can wall up to 63 °C → motor cans + motor-side couplings in ASA | [thermal.md](rnd/thermal.md) |
| A7 roll teeth | superseded: aluminium HTD small pulleys (D13), roll 18T:80T | [belts.md](rnd/belts.md) |
| A8 FEA method + stiffness chain (old item 3) | P2 tets: elbow housing and yaw block were 8× / 2.7× softer than P1 showed (≈ 37 mm at the hand); stiffened to ≈ 12 mm raw | [stiffness.md](rnd/stiffness.md) (new R2b row) |
| A9 balancer | 2:1 tackle + ISO die spring (the old spring could not fit), grooved pulleys, park pin | [balancer.md](rnd/balancer.md) |
| A10 tip cases | quasi-static ZMP cases + D18 anti-tip casters + planner rules | [tip.md](rnd/tip.md) |
| A11 wheels | D17 bolted wheels + chassis ring | BOM |
| A12 dock | bumper ring gap at the pads | rnd/parts/base.py |
| A13 BMS | smart BMS with telemetry; weekly 4.20 V balance charge | BOM, electrical.md |
| A14 CAN load | two channels per arm (≈ 46 % each), base at 250 Hz | electrical.md |
| A15 lift lock | steel rack + steel pawl + belt guards (BOM; CAD next) | BOM |
| A16 contingency | 15 % line in the BOM and the M1 kit | [BOM](rnd/BOM_v4.md), [M1](M1_BENCH_ARM.md) |
| R15 swept spacing | roll and yaw sweeps; found and fixed a shoulder-cap clash at roll 55–95° (since loop 22) | [swept.md](rnd/swept.md) |
| Software S0.2, S0.3, S0.4a, S1 | URDF/MJCF export; LeRobot-format synthetic demos; BC pipeline dry run; DM-J4310 MIT driver, safety supervisor + tests | [SOFTWARE_PLAN.md](SOFTWARE_PLAN.md) |

**Open queue (ordered):**
1. **Hand stiffness, last mile:** the elbow housing is still the largest term (≈ 4 mm at the hand). Thicker variants failed to mesh (gmsh PLC error); fix the mesh, try a 5.5 mm +Y wall and a printed internal frame. Roll link web 4 → 8 mm.
2. **Lift on the D13 stage:** its 20T pulley sits straight on the KX seat with the open belt's pull on it (the same failure class as the wrist roll). Move the lift onto small_stage_v2 with walls in the lift mount; steel rack CAD (A15).
3. **GIM6010 CAN driver** from the SteadyWin manual (blocked here; day 1 of M1). **S0.4b** pretrained checkpoint + open-data slice: needs huggingface.co allowed in this environment (owner decision).

=== FILE: docs/review/triage_2026-11-26.md ===
# External design review #1: Gemini 4 Neon (2026-11-26): triage

The full review is in [gemini4neon_review_2026-11-26.md](gemini4neon_review_2026-11-26.md). It was produced by `rnd/external_review.py`: a 207 kB
packet (scorecard, decisions, 20 reports, params.py, all part CAD) plus 2 renders, sent to `gemini-4-neon`, reasoning high.

**Verdict:** the review is high value. It found a class of problem our checks don't test, **assembly sequence and serviceability**, plus several
safety-electronics gaps. Each finding is triaged below: **V** = verified in the repo, **P** = plausible but not yet checked, **D** = disputed (with reason).

| # | Finding (severity) | Triage | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Trapped bearings / monolithic shafts: bearings can't be installed; no keyed torque transfer shaft→link (critical) | **V** (small_hub: 61804 journals sit between a Ø87 hub and Ø36 pulley flanges) | Queue A1: split hubs/pulleys and bolt them; keys/D-flats; add an **assembly-sequence check** to rnd/integration.py |
| 2 | KX print-in-place: the spring pocket is too small, the balls aren't captured, the pause order is wrong (critical) | P (needs a geometric check) | Queue A2: verify; likely move to a screwed retainer + a real spring pocket (6–8 mm) |
| 3 | KX creep / tooth stress at 55–70 °C under-estimated (major) | P: consistent with loop 29 (thin peak margin) | C1 creep hold at 65 °C / 168 h; thicker teeth; PETG-CF/PA-CF for KX parts |
| 4 | Clock-spring cavities don't exist in CAD; yaw free length ~145 mm not 240; a long CSI FFC is risky; mast/base moving harness missing (critical) | V (cavities are analysis-only; the FFC length point is fair) | Queue A3: model the cavities; re-measure the yaw run; switch to USB2 UVC on a slim cable (reverse the D12 FFC part); mast drag chain |
| 5 | Wrist pitch/roll and head tilt carry bending on actuator shafts (no 61805s in CAD); head tilt 75 % of servo rating (major) | **V** (61805 only in the mass list) | Queue A4: add the wrist bearing pairs; move the head tilt axis under the head COM + passive pivot |
| 6 | E-stop: 0.5 s hold-up, no regen clamp, no pre-charge, 12 V servos bypass the contactor (critical) | V (as designed: SS1 with 0.5 s; no clamp / pre-charge in the plan) | Queue A5: regen clamp + brake resistor, pre-charge, servos downstream, STO review |
| 7 | Thermal model ignores PETG cans + enclosed shells; PETG HDT ~65–70 °C (critical) | P: fair. Our 100 °C was the winding limit, not the PETG limit | Queue A6: PETG-side temperature limit 55 °C, vented cans, aluminium motor plate, bench test |
| 8 | Roll stage 20T in CAD vs 18T in belts/BOM; straight-sided printed teeth; small_hub interlayer shear never FEA'd (critical) | **V** (roll.py uses small_hub() default 20T) | Queue A7: fix teeth; consider aluminium HTD small pulleys on steel stubs; FEA small_hub |
| 9 | FEA: 99.5th-percentile truncation + P1 tets on thin walls hide peaks / under-predict deflection; hand sag likely 8–20 mm (major) | P: fair on method (we report max too) | Queue A8: P2 tets; peak stress at fillets; full-chain compliance (was queue item 3) |
| 10 | Balancer spring can't fit the 135 mm cage; Ø16 ungrooved pulley; arm-detach snap hazard (major) | **V** (balancer.py assumes 105 mm stroke in a 135 mm cage) | Queue A9: re-design the balancer (vertical spring/extension springs/gas strut), grooved pulley, park-lock pin |
| 11 | Tip margin untested for 2.5 kg bimanual + 45° azimuth + braking + human nudge (major) | P | Queue A10: add those cases to twin/mc |
| 12 | Threshold MC saturates the wheel motors (1.00×); KX on wheels could slip; skirt clearance on carpet; deck quarters not tied (major) | V (1.00× is in threshold_mc.md) | Queue A11: drop KX on the wheels (bolt hubs), steel/Al chassis ring, raise skirt clearance |
| 13 | Dock: the bumper_light ring clashes; the frictionless-slide sim; floor screws (major) | **V** (ring R 250–254 at z 43–53, between the pads) | Queue A12: cut the ring at the pads; ramp-baseplate dock; re-sim with wheel friction |
| 14 | BMS undersized / no telemetry; 4.15 V stop prevents balancing; blind-mate XT30 risks (major) | P | Queue A13: smart BMS with telemetry; periodic 4.20 V balance charge; umbilical plug |
| 15 | CAN load ~76 % at 500 Hz (major) | V (numbers from electrical.md) | Queue A14: 250 Hz on base/lift/sensors; bench latency test |
| 16 | Lift ratchet: bonded PETG teeth on Al; drop/pinch hazards; open belt nips (critical) | P | Queue A15: bolted steel rack + metal pawl, or a fail-safe EM brake; belt guards |
| 17 | Printability: bed-edge warping, support-heavy brackets; build time realistically 45–70 h (major) | P | Fold into A1 (flat bolt-together plates) and R10 |
| 18 | Cost: no shipping/tax/contingency; landed $4.5–5.2k (major) | P: fair | Queue A16: 15 % contingency line in the BOM; price the ESTIMATE lines |

**Effect on confidence:** the review shows the CAD is **not yet buildable as drawn** (finding 1). Several sub-systems need a second design pass
(KX socket construction, balancer, lift lock, e-stop electronics). Until queue A1–A6 close, "works on the first full build" is lower than stated
in confidence.md. The staged path (coupons → M1 bench arm) is unchanged, and the reviewer rated it a strength.


=== FILE: docs/rnd/spring.md ===
# Shoulder spring balance (auto-generated by `python -m rnd.run_all`)

6000 random right-arm poses across the full joint ranges (MuJoCo twin, static). A pitch-only balancer τ = K·sin(pitch) (zero-free-length spring + cable: Anglepoise / Herder gravity balancing) is fitted to the arm's own weight.

**Design K = 4.50 N·m** (spring torque at the horizontal arm; `params.arm.spring_k_nm`). A least-squares fit to the bare arm gives 4.54 N·m; a K sweep showed 4.0 minimises the 95th-percentile motor torque for a 0.5 kg typical payload (0.5 kg: 5.2 N·m; 1 kg: 7.3 N·m). The spring preload is screw-adjustable (Anglepoise precedent), so K can be re-tuned per task.

| Motor torque needed at the pitch joint | 95th percentile | Worst pose |
|---|---|---|
| No spring, 1 kg payload | 12.4 N·m | 16.7 N·m |
| Spring, arm only | 4.5 N·m | 8.0 N·m |
| **Spring, 1 kg payload** | **8.9 N·m** | **12.2 N·m** |
| Joint capacity (GIM6010-8 × 3.0 belt), rated / peak | 14.2 | 31.3 |

The spring cancels **39%** of the arm's self-weight torque at the 95th percentile (arm_sizing assumed 85 % everywhere).
Margin with payload: rated / 95th pct **1.60×**, peak / worst pose **2.56×**.

## Every arm joint over the whole workspace (1 kg payload, static, balancer on)

The planar check in [arm sizing](arm_sizing.md) only looks at a straight horizontal arm. That pose never loads the upper-arm yaw, which holds the forearm + payload whenever the elbow is bent and the upper arm is not vertical (on a Franka arm that joint is as strong as the shoulder).

| Joint | Actuator × ratio | Need: 95th pct / worst (N·m) | Rated / peak | Margin rated/p95 | Margin peak/worst |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| shoulder_pitch | GIM6010-8 × 3 | 8.8 / 12.1 | 14.2 / 31.3 | 1.62 | 2.58 ✅ |
| shoulder_roll | GIM6010-8 × 4.44444 | 13.1 / 16.8 | 21.1 / 46.4 | 1.62 | 2.76 ✅ |
| upper_yaw | GIM6010-8 × 2 | 5.3 / 7.0 | 9.5 / 20.9 | 1.79 | 2.97 ✅ |
| elbow | GIM6010-8 × 2 | 6.2 / 7.0 | 9.5 / 20.9 | 1.53 | 2.97 ✅ |
| wrist_pitch | DM-J4310-2EC_V1.1 × 1 | 1.8 / 1.9 | 3.0 / 7.0 | 1.68 | 3.74 ✅ |

**Loop 5 change:** upper-arm yaw DM-J4310 direct (rated/p95 0.65×, ❌) → **GIM6010-8 × 2 belt**. It's cheaper (~$70 per arm), backdrivable, the same part as the other big joints, and adds +0.29 kg per arm. Considered: DM-J4340P direct (1.96×, +70 g, +$29, but 40:1 gearing is barely backdrivable).

=== FILE: docs/rnd/belts.md ===
# Belt reductions (auto-generated by `python -m rnd.run_all`)

HTD 5M, 15 mm, printed pulleys, small pulley on its own bearing hub. **Loop 13 re-rating:** the 650 N/10 mm vendor figure used before is not a working rating. The checks now are: **sustained pull** (95th-percentile workspace holding torque, 1 kg) vs the **working tension 312 N** (Mädler neoprene 5M-15, the conservative published figure), and **peak pull** (actuator peak, short transients; the KX seat slips above it) vs **ultimate 3900 N / 3**. Precedent: Berkeley Blue's belt reductions.

| Joint | Teeth | Pitch Ø small / big | Sustained pull vs working | Peak pull vs ultimate/3 | Teeth in mesh | Centre / belt length |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| shoulder_pitch | 20:60 (3.00:1) | 31.8 / 95.5 | 194 / 312 N → 1.6× ✅ | 691 N → 5.6× ultimate ✅ | 7.0 ✅ | 70 / 354 mm |
| shoulder_roll | 18:80 (4.44:1) | 28.6 / 127.3 | 216 / 312 N → 1.4× ✅ | 768 N → 5.1× ultimate ✅ | 6.1 ✅ | 103.3 / 475 mm |
| upper_yaw | 20:40 (2.00:1) | 31.8 / 63.7 | 176 / 312 N → 1.8× ✅ | 691 N → 5.6× ultimate ✅ | 8.7 ✅ | 80 / 313 mm |
| elbow | 20:40 (2.00:1) | 31.8 / 63.7 | 205 / 312 N → 1.5× ✅ | 691 N → 5.6× ultimate ✅ | 8.5 ✅ | 70 / 294 mm |

- Belt lengths round to stock sizes; eccentric bushings set the tension (loop 10).
- If coupon tests show tooth jump or creep: switch to PU steel-cord HTD 5M (1268 N working, Mädler) at the same geometry, or to 25 mm belts.
- **Rejected (loop 3):** remote elbow motor on a long belt.

![elbow belt stage: cartridge M on its pulley hub, 20T→40T HTD 5M](img/elbow_belt_stage.png)


=== FILE: docs/rnd/kx.md ===
# KX joint interface v1: analysis (auto-generated by `python -m rnd.run_all`)

**Architecture.** A cartridge carries **torque only**. Its output face locks into a **hub** that turns in the bone's own bearing pair, and the motor housing is held by a **torque pin** in a radial slot (precedent: shaft-mounted gearboxes with a torque arm). Bending, axial and crash loads go through the bone's bearings, never through the printed interface or the motor's bearing.

**Seat.** 3 hardened balls on the cartridge; each sits on **two hardened dowel pins** in the hub (a ball on two rods: Maxwell coupling). A **breech-lock collar** with ramped teeth pulls the faces together, and a **spring stack** sets the preload (it rotates on a PTFE thrust washer).

**Design sweep (loop 2):** contact angle β 30° → 20° and balls Ø8 → Ø10 on Ø5 pins (KX74). The same 1.15× peak slip margin then needs 160 N of preload instead of 250 N. Twist effort drops from 4.2–6.3 to 2.1–2.7 N·m, and peak contact stress drops from 4,227 to ~3,550 MPa.

## Geometry

| Size | Flange OD | Ball / PCD | Pins | Contact angle β | Preload | Twist | Collar OD |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| KX74 | 74 | Ø10 / 58 | 6 × Ø5×14 | 20° | 160 N over a 1.0 mm stroke | 35° | 98 |
| KX56 | 56 | Ø8 / 43 | 6 × Ø4×11 | 20° | 140 N over a 1.0 mm stroke | 35° | 78 |
| KX44 | 44 | Ø6 / 31 | 6 × Ø3×8 | 20° | 80 N over a 1.0 mm stroke | 35° | 64 |

## Loads and stresses

| Interface + actuator | Rated / peak (N·m) | Slip torque | Ball-on-pin stress, rated / peak (limit 4,200 MPa) | Same ball in a **printed** groove, rated (PETG yield ~50 MPa) | Twist to lock, greased / dry | Lug shear / tooth bending (PETG) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| KX74 + GIM6010-8 | 5.00 / 11.00 | 12.7 N·m ✅ | 3,226 / 3,554 MPa ✅ | 170 MPa ❌ (3× yield) | 1.9 / 2.6 N·m ✅ | 1.3 / 14 MPa |
| KX56 + DM-J4310-2EC_V1.1 | 3.00 / 7.00 | 8.3 N·m ✅ | 3,555 / 3,933 MPa ✅ | 188 MPa ❌ (4× yield) | 1.3 / 1.7 N·m ✅ | 1.7 / 17 MPa |
| KX44 + STS3215-C018 | 0.98 / 2.94 | 3.4 N·m ✅ | 3,506 / 3,966 MPa ✅ | 185 MPa ❌ (4× yield) | 0.6 / 0.8 N·m ✅ | 1.2 / 12 MPa |

## Socket bearings (one pair per socket)

| Socket | Interface | Bending moment (N·m) | Bearing pair | Max radial load per bearing | Static safety C0/P0 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| shoulder_roll | KX74 | 14.5 | 2 × 61808 | 929 N | 3.9 ✅ |
| upper_yaw | KX74 | 11.9 | 2 × 61808 | 759 N | 4.8 ✅ |
| elbow | KX74 | 7.1 | 2 × 61808 | 456 N | 8.0 ✅ |
| wrist_pitch | KX56 | 1.9 | 2 × 61805 | 141 N | 18.5 ✅ |
| wrist_roll | KX44 | 1.3 | 2 × 61805 | 115 N | 22.6 ✅ |
| drive wheel | KX74 | 3.3 | 2 × 61808 | 261 N | 14.0 ✅ |

## Arm-detach coupling KX-V (loop 7)

The arm comes off at the shoulder-pitch output for carrying (D1). Unlike a joint cartridge, this plane carries the whole arm's bending, so it uses a **V-band clamp**: printed sanitary 3" flanges (stock stainless quick-release clamp fits), 3 steel dowels for location and torque, face contact over the full ring. Precedent: turbo / exhaust V-band joints, spacecraft Marman clamps, tri-clamp fittings.

| Check | Need (static × 1.5) | Capacity | |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bending (lift-off of the face ring) | 21.8 N·m | 40.0 N·m at 1000 N preload | 1.8× ✅ |
| Torque by face friction alone | 18.2 N·m | 10.0 N·m | 0.5× (dowels carry the rest: 7 MPa shear in Ø6 steel) |
| Flange bearing pressure | – | 0.9 MPa on printed PETG | ✅ |

The face is kinematically over-constrained (not a ball seat), so re-attachment repeats to ~0.05–0.1 mm (dowel fit), not microns. That's fine for an arm that re-homes from its absolute joint encoders; a camera check corrects any residual offset.

## Reading the numbers

- **Printed grooves fail.** A steel ball pressed into a printed V-groove at working load reaches several times PETG's yield. It would dent and creep, and the joint would lose its zero. **Dowel-pin grooves are the baseline**, not a fallback. Six Ø3–4 mm hardened pins plus three G25 balls per interface cost well under $1.
- **Slip torque is a feature.** With a defined preload the seat is a **ball-detent overload clutch**: it holds the actuator's peak torque, and beyond that it lets go before a printed bone breaks. It re-seats at the same position (kinematic). A slip past one ball pitch re-indexes by 120°, so the joint must re-home: v1 adds a magnet index in every hub for a Hall check (cartridge electronics item).
- **Ball-on-pin contact stress** stays under the bearing-steel static limit even at the actuators' absolute peak torque.
- **The twist effort is jar-lid territory (about 2–3 N·m).** That is why the collar has two thumb tabs and the ramp gets a smear of grease. Self-locking holds because the 6° ramp is below the friction angle (8.5° greased).
- **Socket bearings:** 61808 / 61805 thin-section pairs (~$2 each, ESTIMATE). Safety ≥ 2 everywhere under the conservative load case.

Assumptions marked ESTIMATE in `rnd/kx_check.py`; contact stresses use the R_eff = √(Ra·Rb) point-contact approximation.

![cartridges M and S, exploded](img/cartridge_m_exploded.png)


=== FILE: docs/rnd/kx_mc.md ===
# KX coupling Monte Carlo (generated by `python -m rnd.kx_mc`)

Model (rnd/kx_check.py): slip torque = P·r_ball / tan β; preload P = k·c. The spring rate is k ≈ P_nom / 3 mm and the
compression is c ≈ 3 mm nominal (D15, loop 40: 6 coil springs; loops 29–39 used 1.5 mm). Each draw (all ESTIMATE until coupon C1 measures them):
- spring rate ×N(1, 0.07): coil-spring tolerance ±10 % at ~1.5σ
- compression error N(0, 0.07 mm): ±0.1 mm on each of the two printed seats (collar tooth, hub step)
- contact angle β = 20° + N(0, 1°): dowel-pin positions in printed holes, ball pocket depth
- PETG creep over 10 years in the preload loop. The elastic loop deflection is 0.06 mm at P_nom (the collar alone is
  0.03 mm by FEA, doubled for hub + lugs), the creep modulus ratio E(10 y)/E is U(0.45, 0.6) at room temperature, and the
  arm joints near a warm motor (task-day 55 °C, rnd/thermal.md) use U(0.30, 0.40)
- ramp friction μ U(0.12, 0.30), for the twist effort

Pass rules:
- slip torque ≥ the actuator's rated (continuous) torque in ≥ 99.9 % of draws. Below rated, the joint would slip in
  normal work.
- Slip below *peak* is reported. That is the designed overload-clutch behaviour, but a high rate means lost position on
  hard moves (the joint-side encoders detect it).
- Twist torque ≤ 3 N·m in ≥ 95 %.

**Worst P(slip < rated) = 0.000%** (rule ≤ 0.1 %). **Worst 95th-percentile twist = 2.9 N·m** (rule ≤ 3).

| Interface | Rated / peak (N·m) | Nominal slip | 0.1 % slip | Mean preload loss (10 y creep + tol.) | P(slip < rated) | P(slip < peak) | Twist p95 (N·m) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| KX74 + GIM6010-8 (warm) | 5.00 / 11.00 | 12.7 | 9.0 | 3.8% | 0.000% | 13.2% | 2.9 |
| KX56 + DM-J4310-2EC_V1.1 (warm) | 3.00 / 7.00 | 8.3 | 5.9 | 3.7% | 0.000% | 9.3% | 1.9 |
| KX44 + STS3215-C018 | 0.98 / 2.94 | 3.4 | 2.5 | 1.8% | 0.000% | 9.3% | 0.9 |

**Reading:** coupon C1 (print-in-place KX socket) measures the real slip torque, twist and re-seat repeatability. These replace the ESTIMATE spreads above.

=== FILE: docs/rnd/thermal.md ===
# Motor thermal check (generated by `python -m rnd.thermal`)

Model (per motor, first order). Copper loss scales with torque², so the steady winding rise is
ΔT_ss = ΔT_RATED · (τ / τ_rated)². τ_rated is the maker's continuous torque, taken to be the torque that gives ΔT_RATED
in still air (ESTIMATE). Heating follows ΔT(t) = ΔT_ss · (1 − e^(−t/TAU)).

Duty cases:
1. **Task day.** The MuJoCo fetch cycle repeated back to back with no rest, in the Monte Carlo worst corner: arms ×1.25,
   joint friction 0.6 N·m, motors at 75 % torque constant (so the same torque needs more current: τ_rated × 0.75), 0.6 kg can.
2. **Worst static hold.** The worst workspace pose with the 1 kg payload, held indefinitely. The result is the time to
   reach the limit.

Ambient 30 °C, limit 100 °C, ΔT at rated 60 K, τ 600 s (all ESTIMATE until the M1 bench arm logs a real motor's temperature).

## 1. Task day: fetch cycle back to back, Monte Carlo worst corner

| Actuator | RMS torque/force | Rated (derated ×0.75) | Winding (enclosed) | Can wall | PETG can (≤ 55 °C) | ASA can (≤ 75 °C) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| pitch | 7.47 | 10.69 | 69 °C ✅ | 63 °C | ❌ | ✅ |
| roll | 1.45 | 15.83 | 41 °C ✅ | 40 °C | ✅ | ✅ |
| yaw | 0.26 | 7.12 | 40 °C ✅ | 40 °C | ✅ | ✅ |
| elbow | 3.17 | 7.12 | 52 °C ✅ | 49 °C | ✅ | ✅ |
| wpitch | 0.43 | 2.25 | 42 °C ✅ | 42 °C | ✅ | ✅ |
| wroll | 0.02 | 0.73 | 40 °C ✅ | 40 °C | ✅ | ✅ |
| lift | 146.56 | 235.85 | 63 °C ✅ | 59 °C | ❌ | ✅ |
| base_x | 7.01 | 98.43 | 40 °C ✅ | 40 °C | ✅ | ✅ |
| base_y | 0.53 | 98.43 | 40 °C ✅ | 40 °C | ✅ | ✅ |

**Hottest motor on a non-stop task day: 69 °C** winding (limit 100 °C); **hottest can wall 63 °C** (A6: enclosed air +10 K). **Decision (A6): motor cans, KX hubs/collars of motor stages and the D13 hubs print in ASA** (Bambu ASA, HDT ≈ 94 °C; the P2S is enclosed, so it prints well); the rest stays PETG. Each can gets a 3 × 8 mm vent slot pair. The M1 bench logs a can-wall thermistor to replace these ESTIMATEs.

## 2. Worst static hold (worst workspace pose, 1 kg payload, nominal motors)

| Joint | Worst static torque (N·m) | Rated (N·m) | Steady temperature | Time to the limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| shoulder_pitch | 12.1 | 14.2 | 74 °C | unlimited ✅ |
| shoulder_roll | 16.8 | 21.1 | 68 °C | unlimited ✅ |
| upper_yaw | 7.0 | 9.5 | 63 °C | unlimited ✅ |
| elbow | 7.0 | 9.5 | 63 °C | unlimited ✅ |
| wrist_pitch | 1.9 | 3.0 | 53 °C | unlimited ✅ |

**A6, can wall in the worst static hold (ASA limit 75 °C):** shoulder_pitch 75 °C (unlimited); shoulder_roll 71 °C (unlimited); upper_yaw 66 °C (unlimited); elbow 66 °C (unlimited); wrist_pitch 59 °C (unlimited).

Every joint can hold its worst pose indefinitely.

=== FILE: docs/rnd/stiffness.md ===
# Hand-deflection compliance chain (generated by `python -m rnd.stiffness`)

The scorecard's hand sag (rnd/bones.py) only bends the carbon tubes. The joint encoders sit on the joint outputs, so
they see and correct belt stretch and KX twist, but not anything that bends or tilts **after** a joint output. This
chain adds those terms for the worst static case: 1 kg at the grasp point, arm straight out horizontally.

1. **Printed structure (FEA).** The four parts that carry the whole arm moment are re-solved with quadratic (P2)
   tetrahedra, which the review asked for (P1 tets are too stiff in bending), and with P1 on the same mesh for the
   ratio. Each part's loaded bearing seats are fitted with a rigid motion (translation t, rotation ω); the hand moves
   by t + ω × r (r = seat → hand). FEA loads are the ×1.5 design cases; the chain uses them ÷ 1.5.
2. **Bearing play.** A 61808 pair with C0 radial clearance (≈ 10 µm each, ESTIMATE) tilts by 2·c/span. Shimmed
   (light axial preload) pairs remove most of it: the chain uses 30 % of the free play for shimmed pairs.
3. **Carbon tubes** (rnd/bones.py) as before.

The total is the static error a calibrated gravity model can largely remove (it is repeatable); the wrist camera closes
the loop for grasping in any case. Target R2b: uncompensated ≤ 6 mm, after the gravity model (assumed to remove 80 %) ≤ 2 mm.

**Static hand deflection, 1 kg straight out: 12.1 mm uncompensated (target ≤ 6), 2.4 mm after a gravity model (target ≤ 2).** ❌

## 1. Printed parts (P2 vs P1 on the same mesh)

| Part | Elements | Seat rotation P1 → P2 (mrad, static) | P2/P1 | Seat translation (mm) | Lever to hand (mm) | Hand (mm) | Von Mises P2 max / 99.5 % (P1 99.5 %) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Upper bone / elbow housing | 30,471 | 1.65 → 9.49 | 5.75 | 0.34 | 436 | 4.48 | 8.7 / 2.2 (0.6) MPa |
| Shoulder pitch bracket | 30,162 | 1.30 → 2.06 | 1.59 | 0.17 | 736 | 1.68 | 4.3 / 2.2 (1.8) MPa |
| Roll link | 24,396 | 1.67 → 2.71 | 1.62 | 0.18 | 736 | 2.17 | 8.2 / 2.6 (1.5) MPa |
| Clevis + yaw block | 22,924 | 0.93 → 1.90 | 2.05 | 0.11 | 736 | 1.51 | 6.6 / 2.3 (0.9) MPa |

## 2. Bearing play (shimmed pairs, 30 % of 10 µm C0 clearance)

| Joint | Span (mm) | Hand (mm) |
|---|---|---|
| pitch (61808 pair) | 34 | 0.13 |
| roll (61808 pair) | 34 | 0.13 |
| yaw (61808 pair) | 34 | 0.13 |
| elbow (61808 + 6708) | 108 | 0.02 |

## 3. Carbon tubes (rnd/bones.py): 1.9 mm

**Reading:** P2 elements roughly double the P1 bending rotations here, as the review said; P1 FEA alone under-states the deflection. The stress margins in fea.md (P1, 99.5th percentile) are re-checked by the P2 column: compare with the 12 MPa across-layer allowable.

=== FILE: docs/rnd/fea.md ===
# FEA of critical printed parts (generated by `python -m rnd.fea`)

Material: printed PETG, E 1.8 GPa, ν 0.38. Allowables:
- 25 MPa in-layer (50 MPa × 0.5 safety)
- **12 MPa across layers**: layer adhesion ×0.5 then safety ×2 (ESTIMATE). Peak von Mises is compared with this
  conservative value, because the stress direction vs the layers isn't resolved here.

Each case lists its loads (worst static ×1.5 dynamic, or actuator peak for belts). Peak stress is reported at the
99.5th percentile of element stresses, to drop single-element singularities at sharp corners.

| Case | Elements | Von Mises (99.5th pct) | Max displacement | Margin vs 12 MPa |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| KX74 collar (locked): teeth at 1.5 × preload (240 N) | 55,317 | 1.4 MPa (max 6.2) | 0.03 mm | 8.7× ✅ |
| Upper bone / elbow housing: forearm load ×1.5 on the elbow bosses | 63,410 | 0.6 MPa (max 3.2) | 0.35 mm | 19.7× ✅ |
| Wheel pod: wheel load 168 N (×2 bumps) + 0.5 g lateral | 72,187 | 0.2 MPa (max 0.4) | 0.03 mm | 53.2× ✅ |
| Shoulder pitch bracket: arm moment 19 N·m + belt peak 691 N | 82,056 | 1.8 MPa (max 4.0) | 0.40 mm | 6.6× ✅ |
| Elbow belt cassette plate: 60 % of the belt peak (415 N) | 17,078 | 1.2 MPa (max 1.6) | 0.04 mm | 9.7× ✅ |
| Roll link: roll belt peak 768 N + arm moment 19 N·m (couple 559 N) | 105,028 | 1.8 MPa (max 4.7) | 0.72 mm | 6.8× ✅ |
| Clevis + yaw block: yaw belt peak 691 N + arm moment 19 N·m (couple 559 N) | 95,027 | 1.1 MPa (max 4.9) | 0.29 mm | 10.6× ✅ |
| Torque hanger (wheel): GIM peak 11 N·m → 367 N on the fork | 34,101 | 2.1 MPa (max 5.4) | 0.08 mm | 5.7× ✅ |

=== FILE: docs/rnd/dexterity.md ===
# Dexterity map, R16 (generated by `python -m rnd.dexterity`)

The reach metric (R1) asks whether the hand reaches a set of targets with any orientation the planner likes. This asks
the harder question a manipulation policy needs: over a table grid in front of the bench arm, what fraction of points
can the hand reach **with a given grasp orientation**, from any approach heading about vertical?

- grid: x 0.25…0.70 m, y −0.40…+0.20 m (5 cm), two heights (5 and 25 cm above the table): 240 points
- grasps: top-down, 45° tilted, side (horizontal); headings 0/90/180/270° about vertical
- IK: the bench env's damped least squares, 8 seeds; success = 5 mm and ≈ 6° orientation error, inside the joint limits
  (sw/bench_env.py builds the arm from rnd/params.py and the twin's joint ranges, so a range change shows up here)
- "across the body" = the points with y > 0 (the other arm's side of the bench)

Targets (set from the loop-32 arm with yaw ±150°: 36 / 69 / 68 / 60 %): no grasp class may drop more than 6 points.

**Joint ranges:** yaw -150…150°, roll -15…100°, pitch -200…60°, elbow -150…0°. **Below target ❌**

| Grasp | Reachable (now) | Target | Loop 32 (yaw ±150°) | Loop 33–39 (yaw ±70°) | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| top-down | 34% | ≥ 30% | 36% | 34% | ✅ |
| 45° angled | 70% | ≥ 63% | 69% | 57% | ✅ |
| side (horizontal) | 66% | ≥ 62% | 68% | 40% | ✅ |
| side, across the body | 54% | ≥ 54% | 60% | 2% | ❌ |

=== FILE: docs/rnd/swept.md ===
# Swept spacing over joint ranges (generated by `python -m rnd.swept`)

The static spacing check (rnd/spacing.py) sees one pose. This one rotates the moving side of a joint through its whole
range and reports the minimum gap to the static side at every step (BRepExtrema), so a part that only collides at
roll 75° or yaw 150° shows up.

Sweeps:
- **shoulder roll** −15…100° (twin.JR): moving = everything on the roll output (roll shaft + 80T, spring hub, clevis and
  yaw block, yaw cartridge, yaw shaft, upper bone top); static = roll link, roll cartridge, shoulder cap, ferrule.
- **upper-arm yaw** ±155° (the hard stops): moving = yaw shaft + 40T + upper bone / elbow housing top; static = yaw block,
  yaw cartridge and band, spring ring. Checks the stop pin hits only its slot ends.

Target: moving ≥ 2 mm (hard 1 mm), as in R15; pairs R15 classes as running (bearing-set gaps) use ≥ 0.3 mm.

**Minimum gap over the sweep: roll 0.7 mm, yaw 2.0 mm. Pairs under target: 0; under the hard limit: 0.**

| Joint | Moving part | Static part | Kind | Min gap (mm) | Target / hard | at angle | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

=== FILE: docs/rnd/spacing.md ===
# Spacing check, R15 (generated by `python -m rnd.spacing`)

Minimum distance (BRepExtrema) between every pair of parts in a module whose bounding boxes come within 2 mm. Each
pair is classified, and the first matching rule wins:

- **mate:** fastened, bonded, fused or nested by design (a motor in its can, a collar on its hub, a part bolted to a
  bracket, deck quarters, a bezel over the screen). No spacing rule.
- **running:** relative motion across a clearance set by a bearing, shim or spacer (axial gaps beside bearings, a pin in
  a guide slot). Target ≥ 0.3 mm.
- **belt:** a belt versus anything other than its own pulleys. Target ≥ 3 mm (hard 1 mm).
- **moving:** other pairs in relative motion. Target ≥ 2 mm (hard 1 mm).
- **static:** everything else. Target ≥ 1 mm (hard 0.5 mm).

Swept checks over the joint ranges are separate (`swept()`): the elbow over −150…0°, the gripper jaw 0…45°, the head
over tilt ±30°, the KX collar twist 0…35°.

**Pairs under target: 12; under the hard limit: 0.**

| Module | Part A | Part B | Kind | Gap (mm) | Target / hard | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| roll/yaw | yaw_shaft | yaw_stop_pin | running | 0.3 | 0.3 / 0.2 | ⚠️ target |
| arm | forearm | wrist_can | static | 0.8 | 1.0 / 0.5 | ⚠️ target |
| arm | e_collar | e_shaft40 | static | 0.83 | 1.0 / 0.5 | ⚠️ target |
| upper | handle | pan_sleeve | static | 0.9 | 1.0 / 0.5 | ⚠️ target |
| arm | e_shaft40 | forearm | static | 1.0 | 1.0 / 0.5 | ⚠️ target |
| base | w90_hub | w90_pod | static | 1.0 | 1.0 / 0.5 | ⚠️ target |
| base | w180_hub | w180_pod | static | 1.0 | 1.0 / 0.5 | ⚠️ target |
| base | w270_hub | w270_pod | static | 1.0 | 1.0 / 0.5 | ⚠️ target |
| arm | e_cassette | e_bigpulley | moving | 1.3 | 2.0 / 1.0 | ⚠️ target |
| roll/yaw | yaw_block | yaw_shaft | moving | 2.0 | 2.0 / 1.0 | ⚠️ target |

=== FILE: docs/rnd/assembly_seq.md ===
# Assembly-sequence check (generated by `python -m rnd.assembly_seq`)

The clash checks prove that parts don't overlap; they don't prove that the parts can be put together. This checks two
things for every drive shaft:

1. **Bearing slide-on.** Each bearing (bore d) must reach its journal from at least one end of the *same printed piece*
   without passing a section wider than d/2 (radius profile sampled every 0.5 mm along the shaft axis).
2. **Shaft insertion.** A one-piece shaft carrying an element wider than the bearing seats of the walls it passes
   through (an integral pulley, flange or ferrule between or beyond two walls of a one-piece link) can't be put in.
   The shaft must be split (bolted pulley / flange), or the link must be split.

**6/6 drive shafts can be assembled as drawn.**

| Shaft | Bearings that can slide on | Insertion | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| pitch shaft (60T split on a double-D; balancer disc + ferrule outboard) | z -53.5…-47.5 (Ø40): ✅ −Z end; z -19.5…-13.5 (Ø40): ✅ −Z end | ✅ | ✅ |
| roll shaft (80T split on a hex; clock-spring hub on the +X end) | z -16.5…-10.5 (Ø40): ✅ −Z end; z 17.5…23.5 (Ø40): ✅ −Z end | ✅ | ✅ |
| yaw shaft + flange (40T split on a hex) | z -72.1…-66.1 (Ø40): ✅ +Z end; z -106.1…-100.1 (Ø40): ✅ +Z end | ✅ | ✅ |
| elbow shaft (40T split on a double-D; 2 × 61808 + 2 × 6708) | z 4.5…10.5 (Ø40): ✅ −Z end; z 38.5…43.7 (Ø40): ✅ −Z end; z -57.5…-50.5 (Ø40): ✅ −Z end; z 50.5…57.5 (Ø40): ✅ −Z end | ✅ | ✅ |
| D13 small stage v2 (steel Ø12 shaft; pulley, coupling, bearings separate): pitch, roll, yaw, elbow | z -30.5…-25.5 (Ø12): ✅ −Z end; z -63.5…-58.5 (Ø12): ✅ −Z end | ✅ | ✅ |
| wheel shaft + flange (×4) | z -44…-37 (Ø40): ✅ +Z end; z -39…-32 (Ø40): ✅ +Z end | ✅ | ✅ |

=== FILE: docs/rnd/integration.md ===
# Load-path integration check (generated by `python -m rnd.integration`)

Clash and printability checks miss parts that *don't touch*. This finds load paths that end in air:
1. **Torque reaction.** Every cartridge can (M/D/S) has a steel torque pin sticking out of its rear floor. The pin's tip
   must land in structure (a radial slot), or the motor housing spins instead of the joint.
2. **Bearing support.** Every small-pulley hub runs on two 61804 journals. Each journal must sit in a structural seat
   (an eccentric bushing in a wall or plate).

3. **Clashes inside a module.** The whole-robot check (`assembly_check`) skips pairs inside one sub-module, so overlaps
   > 50 mm³ inside the arm, roll/yaw, shoulder and hand modules are listed here. Interfaces that are meant to be fused,
   glued or meshed (`FUSED`) are excluded.

A pin counts as reacted when its tip is in clear space and structure sits on both tangential sides of it (a slot or fork).

**Torque reaction: 21/21 cartridge pins land in structure.**

| Check | Part | Result | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| torque pin | w0_can | ✅ | w0_torque_hanger |
| torque pin | w90_can | ✅ | w90_torque_hanger |
| torque pin | w180_can | ✅ | w180_torque_hanger |
| torque pin | w270_can | ✅ | w270_torque_hanger |
| torque pin | lift_can | ✅ | lift_mount |
| torque pin | pan_sleeve | ✅ | head_bracket |
| torque pin | tilt_sleeve | ✅ | yoke |
| torque pin | l_sh_can | ✅ | l_sh_bracket |
| torque pin | l_roll_roll_can | ✅ | l_roll_roll_link |
| torque pin | l_roll_yaw_can | ✅ | l_roll_yaw_block |
| torque pin | l_arm_e_can | ✅ | l_arm_upper_bone |
| torque pin | l_arm_wrist_can | ✅ | l_arm_forearm |
| torque pin | l_arm_hand_wr_sleeve | ✅ | l_arm_hand_palm |
