=== FILE: docs/rnd/harness.md ===
# Cable routing across the joints (generated by `python -m rnd.harness`)

The question: can the arm bundle cross every joint over its full range without a slip ring? Two methods are checked per
joint (all cable data ESTIMATE until the datasheets are in hand):

1. **Torsion through the hollow shaft.** The bundle runs along the axis and twists. A torsion-rated robot cable allows
   about ±360°/m (TORSION_DEG_PER_M), so the free axial length L must be ≥ (range / 2) / 360 m.
2. **Clock-spring spiral** (as in a car's steering-wheel clock spring). The bundle lies in a flat spiral in an annular
   cavity r_in … r_out around the axis. Rotation winds or unwinds it, so the cable bends but never twists. The capacity
   for a spiral of length S is Δθ = S·(1/r_in − 1/r_out) rad (wound tight on r_in ↔ loose on r_out). It needs
   r_in ≥ the bend radius of the stiffest cable (10 × OD for continuous flex) and a cavity of the bundle's height.

The joint ranges come from the twin (rnd/twin.py JR). The free lengths and the cavity sizes are read off the CAD
(module dimensions; ESTIMATE ± 10 mm).

**Joints the arm bundle can cross: 6/6 with D12** (CSI flat-flex through the wrist + slim Ø2.2 USB from the forearm); 2/6 with the camera's stock Ø3.8 USB cable.

| Joint | Range | Torsion: free length / needed | Spiral cavity r_in–r_out (mm) | Bend r (10×OD) | Spiral S / capacity | Ribbon build / width | Method | Stock USB |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| shoulder pitch | 260° | 200 / 361 mm ❌ | 22–60 | 22 mm | 0.60 m / 990° (need 312°) | 9.5 mm / 7.8 mm | clock spring ✅ | ✅ 332° |
| shoulder roll | 115° | 100 / 160 mm ❌ | 22.0–38.0 | 22 mm | 0.25 m / 274° (need 138°) | 4.0 mm / 7.8 mm | clock spring ✅ | ❌ 0° |
| upper-arm yaw | 300° | 0 / 417 mm ❌ | 22.5–40.0 | 22 mm | 0.40 m / 446° (need 360°) | 6.2 mm / 7.8 mm | clock spring ✅ | ❌ 30° |
| elbow | 150° | 130 / 208 mm ❌ | 22–50 | 22 mm | 0.60 m / 875° (need 180°) | 9.5 mm / 11.4 mm | clock spring ✅ | ✅ 217° |
| wrist pitch | 220° | 60 / 306 mm ❌ | 16–30 | 12 mm | 0.40 m / 668° (need 264°) | 4.8 mm / — | clock spring ✅ | ❌ 0° |
| wrist roll | 360° | 30 / 500 mm ❌ | 14–24 | 12 mm | 0.35 m / 597° (need 432°) | 4.8 mm / — | clock spring ✅ | ❌ 0° |

**Findings (loop 32):**
- **No joint can rely on torsion through its bore.** The free axial lengths are 30–200 mm, and the ranges need 160–500 mm at ±360°/m. The old electrical note ('cables through the hollow shafts, no slip rings') needs **clock-spring spirals** at each joint.
- **The camera's stock Ø3.8 USB cable** needs a 38 mm bend radius and fits through none of the wrist joints. **D12:**
  - a MIPI-CSI camera on a thin FFC through both wrist joints
  - a CSI→USB (UVC) bridge board on the forearm
  - a slim 4-core high-flex USB cable (OD ≤ 2.2 mm) from there on
- **Loop 40: roll and yaw cassettes are now real CAD** (external review finding 4). Yaw is back to **±150°** (loop 33 had cut it to ±70°, which cost side grasps 68 → 40 %):
  - **yaw:** a 9.5 mm cavity between the split 40T pulley's hub (rotating inner wall, r 22.5) and the bottom yaw plate, static ring r 40 screwed to the plate; the ribbon's inner end enters the yaw-shaft bore and runs down the neck
  - **roll:** a cassette on the +X end of the roll shaft, outboard of the clevis arm; the static ring hangs on a bar that runs inboard at z ≈ 0 (a direction the roll output never sweeps) to the roll-link boss
  - **D14:** the elbow sensor node makes 12 V and drives the TTL servo bus, so only 24 V + CAN + USB (a 7.8 mm flat ribbon) cross pitch, roll and yaw
- The ribbon: the round cables side by side on a PET/Kapton carrier tape, wound as in a car's clock spring (cable bends, never twists).

## Harness kit (pre-made, one per arm + torso + base)
| Segment | Contents | Length (ESTIMATE) | Ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| Torso e-tray → shoulder detach plug | 24 V pair, CAN pair, 12 V/TTL, USB | 0.45 m + 0.6 m pitch spiral | XT30 + 6-pin pogo / USB-C |
| Detach plug → elbow sensor node | same | 0.40 m + 3 × 0.6 m spirals (roll, yaw, elbow) | daisy-chain JST-GH / VH at each cartridge |
| Elbow node → hand | 12 V/TTL, I²C, camera (FFC per option 1) | 0.35 m + 2 × 0.3 m spirals (wrist) | JST-GH / FFC |
| Mast: torso → head | 24 V (F 1 A), CAN A, TTL 3 | 0.75 m in a cable chain along the lift travel | JST-GH |
| Base ↔ upper coupling | XT60 + 6-pin pogo (CAN A, e-stop loop) | 0.3 m | blind-mate |

=== FILE: docs/rnd/balancer.md ===
# Shoulder balancer (auto-generated by `python -m rnd.run_all`)

Zero-free-length cable-spring balancer. Pulley A sits 45–65 mm above the pitch axis in a slot; pin B sits on a lever on the pitch shaft 30 mm from the axis. A cable runs B → A → (2:1 tackle) → a caged die spring in the torso, giving τ = k·a·b·sin(pitch) at every angle. Precedent: the Anglepoise lamp, Herder's spring balancers, French & Widden cable balancers.

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---|
| Design K (params) | 4.5 N·m |
| Spring rate k = K/(a·b) | **3.00 N/mm** |
| Max spring force (arm hanging) | 239 N |
| Cable span A–B, max (= zero-free-length spring travel without a tackle) | 80 mm; energy 9.6 J |
| **A9: 2:1 tackle → spring rate / deflection / force** | **12 N/mm / 40 mm / 479 N** |
| Die spring L0 115 mm: deflection 35% of L0 (limit 40%); cage 135 mm | ✅ |
| Law error from the Ø11 mm pulley (max over −200…60°) | 0.49 N·m (11% of K) |
| K adjustment by sliding A (45–65 mm) | 4.0–5.8 N·m |
| Cable safety (2 mm UHMWPE, ESTIMATE 2000 N) | 8× |

**Spring (A9, loop 40):** ISO 10243 light-load (green) die spring, Ø25 hole / Ø12.5 rod, L0 115 mm (ESTIMATE rate ≈ 12 N/mm; pick the catalogue rate ≥ 4·k). The loop-7 compression spring (3 N/mm over 85 mm, ≈ 11 J) would have been ≈ 210 mm long and never fitted the 135 mm cage (external review finding 10). The moving pulley halves the travel; die springs store the energy in about half the length. Cable: B → U604 pulley A → U604 moving pulley on the spring plate → anchor screw on the cage.
**Detach hazard (A9):** with the arm off, the spring torque K·sin(pitch) is unopposed. Detach and attach only with the arm hanging (pitch 0, where the torque is zero), with the **park pin** in: a Ø6 pin through the bracket wall into a hole in the balancer disc at pitch 0. The pitch motor holds the shaft as well while powered.
**Failure mode:** with the cable cut, the arm is simply unbalanced; the pitch motor still holds it (see the workspace margins without the spring in spring.md).

![shoulder pitch module](img/shoulder_pitch_v0.png)


=== FILE: docs/rnd/electrical.md ===
# Electrical architecture (auto-generated by `python -m rnd.electrical`)

## Power distribution (24 V class, 6S Li-ion)

```
6S 10 Ah pack (BMS ≥ 30 A cont.) ─ XT60 ─ MAIN FUSE 40 A ─┬─ LOGIC branch (always on): 5 V 5 A DC-DC → Pi 5, cameras, lidar, CAN/TTL adapters
                                                         └─ PRE-CHARGE (22 Ω 25 W, 0.5 s) ∥ E-STOP CONTACTOR (coil through: torso + base mushrooms, watchdog relay)
                                                              ├─ REGEN CLAMP on the motor bus: 26.5 V shunt (MOSFET + 10 Ω 50 W) + 33 V TVS (A5)
                                                              ├─ 12 V 10 A DC-DC → STS3215 servos (A5: now downstream of the contactor)
                                                              ├─ F 20 A → base: 4 wheel cartridges (CAN bus A)
                                                              ├─ F 10 A → lift cartridge + pawl solenoid (CAN bus A)
                                                              ├─ F 15 A → left arm via detach plug (CAN bus B, TTL bus 1)
                                                              └─ F 15 A → right arm via detach plug (CAN bus C, TTL bus 2)
LOGIC 24 V ─ F 1 A ─ D11 FACE SCREEN (ESP32-S3-Touch-LCD-4.3B, 7–36 V input; CAN A drop at the head; isolated DI senses the e-stop loop)
DOCK PADS (skirt, 45°) ─ ideal-diode reverse-polarity guard ─ F 7.5 A ─ BMS charge port   (charger 25.2 V CC/CV, 5 A)
```

**Docking charge path (loop 27):**
- The dock's output is off until the robot answers on the ID pad: the base sensor node pulls a coded load, the dock's ESP32-C3 checks it, then closes its MOSFET.
- The robot may run while it charges (idle ≈ 33 W ≈ 1.4 A of the 5 A), so a stock charger could never see current fall to its end-of-charge level. The robot therefore tells the dock to stop at 4.15 V/cell over the ID line (from BMS telemetry).
- **Check before buying** whether the 6S BMS is common-port (charge and load on one port) or split-port; the wiring above assumes common-port (ESTIMATE).

| Branch | Worst sustained bus current (A), for fuse/wire sizing | Peak (A) | Fuse (A, slow-blow) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Base: 4 wheel cartridges | 2.2 | 28.8 | 20 |
| Left arm: 4 GIM + DM-J4310 | 11.4 | 33.3 | 15 |
| Right arm: 4 GIM + DM-J4310 | 11.4 | 33.3 | 15 |
| Lift cartridge + pawl solenoid | 2.0 | 9.3 | 10 |
| 12 V DC-DC → 6 × STS3215 (wrists, grippers, head) | 2.9 | 5.8 | 7.5 |
| 5 V DC-DC → Pi 5, cameras, lidar, CAN/TTL adapters | 2.0 | 2.6 | 5 |
| Reserved: 24 → 19 V 3 A DC-DC → Jetson-class AI computer (D10, AI phase) | 0.8 | 1.2 | 3 |
| **Pack** | **32.6** | **69** (diversity 0.6) | main 40 |

Typical draw is far lower: the duty-cycle power budget averages **55 W ≈ 2.5 A** (runtime 3.4 h on 222 Wh).

**Battery requirement (new finding):** the pack's BMS must allow **≥ 30 A continuous and about 69 A for ~1 s**. Cheap 6S packs often have 10–15 A BMS boards (components.yaml), which would trip on arm or threshold peaks. Buy a scooter or power-tool class pack, or build one around a 40 A BMS.

## E-stop behaviour (A5, loop 40: external review finding 6)
- **Two-channel mushrooms (torso back, base rear).** Channel 1 opens the contactor coil loop directly (hardware, no software in the path). Channel 2 tells the Pi and the sensor nodes, which command a controlled stop. Motor power drops; logic stays up to log and report.
- **Stop category:** loop 39 had a *safe stop 1* with a 0.5 s capacitor hold-up. A capacitor delay is not a dependable timer, so the **default is now category 0** (power cut at once). The arms go limp; the balancer and belt friction slow the fall (≈ 0.3 s to the stop at the hanging pose; a 1 kg payload is released by the gripper's own back-drive). SS1 can return later with a rated delay relay.
- **Everything that moves is downstream of the contactor:** loop 39 fed the 12 V servo converter from the logic branch, so the wrists, grippers and head kept power in an e-stop. It now sits after the contactor.
- **Pre-charge:** 13 motor drivers put several mF on the bus. Closing straight onto them welds contactor tips over time, so a 22 Ω resistor charges the bus for 0.5 s first (the reset button starts a timer relay).
- **Regen clamp:** the GIM6010 drivers are rated to 28 V, and a full pack is 25.2 V. A decelerating or back-driven arm pumps the bus up when the BMS won't take charge (full pack) or after the contactor opens. A 26.5 V shunt regulator (comparator, MOSFET, 10 Ω 50 W aluminium resistor) plus a 33 V TVS hold the bus. **On the M1 bench this is mandatory:** a bench power supply can't sink current at all.
- **Watchdog:** the Pi's control loop toggles a watchdog input on the e-stop relay board; if the loop stalls for 50 ms the contactor opens (SW2).
- **Torso:** the lift pawl is spring-applied, so it drops in and the torso holds (A15 reviews the pawl itself).
- Not a certified safety function. The bench arm runs inside a marked keep-out zone until SW2 is measured.

## Data buses
| Bus | Members | Rate | Load at 500 Hz |
|---|---|---|---|
| CAN A | 4 wheels + lift + base sensor node + D11 screen (≤ 50 Hz) | 1 Mbps, **250 Hz** | 39% |
| CAN B1 / C1 (one per arm) | shoulder pitch, roll, yaw + the encoder frame of the elbow node | 1 Mbps, 500 Hz | 46% |
| CAN B2 / C2 (one per arm) | elbow + DM-J4310 wrist pitch + the elbow node (tactile/ToF frame, TTL bridge for wrist roll + gripper, D14) | 1 Mbps, 500 Hz | 46% |
| TTL 3 | head pan + tilt (torso node) | 1 Mbps half-duplex | light |
| *Loop 39 layout, for reference* | *one bus per arm: 5 motors + node at 500 Hz (with D14's bridge)* | | *91%: too high (review finding 15)* |

**A14 (loop 40):** each arm gets two CAN channels (≈ 45 % each, room for retries and jitter) and the base runs at 250 Hz. Five channels in all: a 2-channel CAN HAT on the Pi + 3 USB-CAN adapters (candleLight class). **M1 needs 2 channels** (one arm). Load model: 2 frames per motor per cycle (command + reply) and node frames, ≈ 130 bit per 8-byte frame with stuffing.
| USB3 | head RGB-D (Gemini 2), 2 wrist cameras, lidar | – | Pi 5 has 2 × USB3; use a powered USB3 hub |

## Connectors
| Interface | Power | Signal |
|---|---|---|
| Arm detach plug (blind-mate, floats in a printed mount beside the V-band) | XT30 (24 V, 15 A branch) | 6-pin 2 mm pogo block: CAN1 H/L, CAN2 H/L, GND, ID strap (loop 40: D14 + A14; 12 V and TTL are made in the arm) |
| Upper ↔ base coupling (under the cam lever) | XT60 | 6-pin pogo: CAN A H/L, e-stop loop ×2, 5 V sense, GND |
| Battery slide-out | XT60 (keyed) | balance lead to the charger only |
| Dock contacts (skirt at 45°, flush brass pads) | + at 60 mm, − at 100 mm, 60 × 12 mm pads, 5 A | ID pad at 40 mm → base sensor node (handshake, stop-charge command) |
| Every joint cartridge | 2-pin JST-VH (24 V) or GH (12 V) | 4-pin JST-GH CAN/TTL daisy chain |

**Cable routing (loop 32, rnd/harness.py):** torsion through the short shaft bores can't absorb the joint ranges (cable rated ~±360°/m). Every joint crossing uses a **clock-spring spiral** in an annular cavity around the joint axis. No slip rings. D12 keeps the stiffest cable at Ø2.2 mm: the wrist camera is MIPI-CSI on a flat-flex through the wrist, with a USB bridge on the forearm. Loop 40: yaw back to ±150° with a real clock-spring cassette; **D14:** the elbow node makes 12 V and drives the TTL servo bus, so only 24 V, CAN (two pairs, A14) and USB cross the shoulder joints. See [harness.md](harness.md).

=== FILE: docs/rnd/accuracy.md ===
# Hand positioning accuracy budget (auto-generated by `python -m rnd.accuracy`)

Static hand error at 0.6 m reach, root-sum-square of the per-joint angular errors × lever arm, plus bone and
mast bending. Error sources (ESTIMATEs, refine with the coupon/bench tests):
- motor encoder resolution (GIM: 14-bit on the motor, ×reduction)
- planetary backlash inside the GIM (≈ 0.2° at its output, typical for small planetary QDDs) ÷ belt ratio
- belt backlash/tooth play (HTD 5M ≈ 0.15° at the joint) and belt stretch under the holding load
- KX seat repeatability (≈ 0.01° kinematic; negligible), bone bending (rnd/bones.py), mast sway (rnd/mast.py)
A **joint-side absolute encoder** (AS5048A, 14-bit, on the joint hub) measures *after* the gearbox and belt, so the
servo loop removes backlash and belt stretch from the static error. What remains is the encoder (±0.05° after
calibration) and the unmeasured bending.

| Joint | Without joint encoders: error (°) → at hand (mm) | With joint-side encoders |
|---|---|---|
| shoulder_pitch | 0.20° → 2.1 mm | 0.05° → 0.5 mm |
| shoulder_roll | 0.21° → 2.2 mm | 0.05° → 0.5 mm |
| upper_yaw | 0.25° → 1.9 mm | 0.05° → 0.4 mm |
| elbow | 0.25° → 1.9 mm | 0.05° → 0.4 mm |
| wrist_pitch | 0.05° → 0.1 mm | 0.05° → 0.1 mm |
| **Joint RSS** | **4.0 mm** | **0.9 mm** |

Plus unmeasured bending: bones 1.9 mm, mast 1.5 mm (worst static, 1 kg).
The wrist camera closes the last loop on the object, so those terms matter for blind or fast moves, not for visually-servoed grasps.

**Decision (D9):** fit AS5048A joint-side encoders on the 8 arm belt joints and the lift. That's about $140 including 3 small CAN encoder nodes. Precedent: link-side encoders on Franka and other research arms.

=== FILE: docs/SOFTWARE_PLAN.md ===
# Pip v4: software track (owner, 2026-11-26)

**Scope:** software for the robot, starting with the **M1 bench arm** (one arm on a stand). It runs alongside the hardware
R&D loop (see RND_PLAN §1 for the alternation and the efficiency rules). Everything is built from `rnd/params.py`, so the
software and the hardware design stay in sync.

**Strategy (the speed lever is data):** don't train from scratch. Start from a **pretrained open policy**, **co-train with
open robot datasets**, add **synthetic demonstrations from our own MuJoCo twin**, and spend real teleoperation demos only on
the gap. Design every interface to look like the large open datasets, so their data and checkpoints transfer:

| Choice | Convention we adopt | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Action space | **End-effector pose deltas (6D) + gripper**, 10–15 Hz policy rate, chunked; joint targets come from our IK | DROID / Open X-Embodiment / most VLA checkpoints use EEF-delta actions. Our 6-DOF + gripper arm differs from every dataset robot in joint space, but not in EEF space |
| Cameras | **Wrist camera + one exterior camera** (+ the head RGB-D on the full robot), 640×480-class, 15–30 fps | DROID's layout (wrist + exterior) |
| Data format | **LeRobot dataset format** (parquet + mp4) | The common interchange format; LeRobot has loaders for the hub datasets and training scripts for ACT, Diffusion, SmolVLA, π0-family |
| Language | One instruction string per episode | Needed for VLA fine-tuning and multi-task |

## Open resources to leverage (shortlist; S3.1 benchmarks them on our task)

- **Pretrained policies:**
  - π0 / π0.5 family (Physical Intelligence `openpi`)
  - SmolVLA (Hugging Face; small, trains on one GPU; pretrained on community LeRobot data)
  - NVIDIA GR00T N-series
  - OpenVLA / OpenVLA-OFT (7B; trained on 970k Open-X trajectories; fine-tuned with 10–150 task demos)
- **Datasets for co-training:**
  - Open X-Embodiment (≈1M trajectories, 22 robots)
  - DROID (Franka, wrist + exterior cameras, EEF actions: the closest match to our convention)
  - BridgeData V2
  - LeRobot hub community datasets (SO-100/SO-101 arms: cheap printed arms like ours)
  - large 2025–26 releases (e.g. AgiBot World-class bimanual data, for the later two-arm phase)
- **Human video:** egocentric glasses data for co-training later (EgoMimic-style). Pip's head camera sits near human eye height, which helps.
- **Caveats (checked in S3):**
  - Fine-tuning a generalist on few demos can cause forgetting, so co-fine-tune with a slice of open data.
  - A checkpoint's action-space conventions (frames, scaling, gripper sign) must be matched exactly before running it on hardware (deployment-safety literature, 2026).

## Phases and deliverables

| Phase | Deliverable | Needs hardware? |
|---|---|---|
| **S0.1** ✅ | `sw/bench_env.py`: MuJoCo bench-arm environment (one arm on a stand, table, objects; wrist + exterior cameras; EEF-delta action interface via our IK), gymnasium API | no |
| **S0.2** ✅ (loop 40) | Robot description export: `sw/export_description.py` → URDF + MJCF + joints.json (limits, ratings) from the same model as the sims; CAD meshes still to add | no |
| **S0.3** ✅ (loop 40) | `sw/gen_demos.py`: scripted expert (the twin's IK pick-and-place planner), domain-randomised (lighting, textures, object pose, camera jitter) → **synthetic demos in LeRobot format** | no |
| **S0.4a** ✅ (loop 40) | `sw/train_bc.py`: dataset → decode → train a small BC policy from scratch on CPU → closed-loop eval in the twin ([train_bc.md](sw/train_bc.md)) | no |
| **S0.4b** ⛔ | Pretrained small policy (SmolVLA / π0-family) co-trained on synthetic + an open-dataset slice. **Blocked: this environment's network policy refuses huggingface.co** (owner decision to allow it) | no (GPU later) |
| **S1** (partly ready, loop 40) | `sw/drivers/mit.py` DM-J4310 MIT frames ✅ tested; `sw/drivers/gim.py` interface (GIM protocol from the manual on day 1: the manual is blocked here). Bench bring-up: drivers (GIM6010 CAN, DM-J4310 MIT mode, STS3215 TTL, RP2040 encoder nodes), 500 Hz control loop, calibration, gravity compensation with the balancer | M1 kit |
| **S1-safety** ✅ software (loop 40) | `sw/safety.py` + `sw/test_s1.py` (fault injection in the twin). Safety supervisor (independent of the policy): torque/speed/workspace limits, watchdog, e-stop → torque-off | M1 kit |
| **S2** | Teleop and recording: EEF teleop (leader arm through IK, or VR hand tracking); LeRobot recorder | M1 kit + leader arm |
| **S3.1** | Checkpoint shortlist benchmark on the bench task (zero-shot / 10 / 50 demos) | GPU (owned or rented) |
| **S3.2** | First skill: pick-and-place a cup | GPU |
| **S4** | Full robot: ROS 2 Nav2 + lidar SLAM, lift, bimanual, docking (AprilTag + V-dock), D11 screen UI | full robot |

## Software targets (measurable, scorecard rows once they exist)

| ID | Target |
|---|---|
| SW1 | Control loop 500 Hz on the Pi 5, p99 jitter < 1 ms, with the cameras streaming |
| SW2 | E-stop or watchdog → all torque off in < 50 ms; policy output always passes the safety supervisor |
| SW3 | Teleop end-to-end latency < 100 ms |
| SW4 | First skill (pick-and-place cup) ≥ 80 % success with **≤ 50 real demos** (from-scratch baseline ≈ 200) |
| SW5 | Policy inference ≥ 10 Hz on the Jetson (Orin Nano Super class) |
| SW6 | Sim pipeline: ≥ 500 synthetic demos/hour on this machine; sim success of the scripted expert ≥ 95 % |


=== FILE: docs/sw/s1_tests.md ===
# S1 software tests (generated by `python -m sw.test_s1`)

1. MIT frames: pack → unpack round trip within one quantisation step; special frames.
2. Safety supervisor against the bench twin with injected faults: a wild policy (random targets far outside the
   limits and the workspace), a stalled loop (watchdog), an e-stop, an over-temperature report. The arm must never leave
   its limits or the workspace box, and every fault must stop it within one cycle.
3. Virtual-bus round trip for the DM-J4310 driver.

- MIT pack/unpack: worst error 0.50 quantisation steps over 2000 random commands ✅
- DM-J4310 driver on a virtual bus: enable, command, feedback ✅
- Safety supervisor vs a random policy: 3000 cycles, 0 limit/workspace violations; watchdog, e-stop, over-temperature and motor-error faults each stop within one cycle ✅ (overrides: {'joint limit clamp': 2967, 'velocity clamp': 3000, 'torque clamp': 3000, 'watchdog': 1, 'e-stop latched': 1, 'over temperature': 1, 'motor error flag': 1})

=== FILE: docs/sw/train_bc.md ===
# S0.4a training-pipeline dry run (generated by `python -m sw.train_bc`)

What it proves: the whole loop works end to end on our own data format before any GPU time is bought:
LeRobot-format dataset (sw/gen_demos.py) → decode videos → train a policy → run it **closed-loop** in the bench twin →
success rate. The policy is deliberately small (a CNN on both cameras at 64 px + the proprio state → a 4-step action
chunk, behaviour cloning with an L1 loss, ACT-style chunking with temporal ensembling). It is a pipeline test, not the
policy we will deploy: S3.1 benchmarks pretrained checkpoints (π0 / SmolVLA / OpenVLA-OFT) on the same data.

S0.4b (pretrained checkpoint + open-data slice) is blocked here: this environment's network policy refuses huggingface.co.

**Data:** 200 synthetic episodes, 13,885 frames (90/10 episode split). Load + decode 39 s; 30 epochs on 4 CPU threads in 14.1 min.

**Result:** validation L1 (normalised) 0.114; **closed-loop success in the twin 10%** over 20 unseen cube poses.

| Epoch | Train L1 | Val L1 |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0.387 | 0.287 |
| 5 | 0.212 | 0.207 |
| 10 | 0.161 | 0.167 |
| 15 | 0.133 | 0.141 |
| 20 | 0.117 | 0.130 |
| 25 | 0.103 | 0.119 |
| 29 | 0.096 | 0.114 |

=== FILE: docs/COUPONS.md ===
# Coupon round C1: test prints before buying hardware (generated by `python -m rnd.coupons`)

**Why:** these are the riskiest *new* mechanisms in the design (see the confidence assessment in RND_LOG). They cost only filament. Print them first and record the measurements below; each result updates the named parameter, and the hourly R&D loop re-runs every check.

**Total:** 274 g, 22.1 h with PrusaSlicer's generic profile ≈ **7.8 h on the P2S** (ESTIMATE).

STLs: `rnd/out/coupons/`.

| Coupon | Material | Generic time (h) | Filament (g) | How to print |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1_kx74_socket_and_collar | PETG | 6.0 | 85 | D15 screwed socket: print hub, collar and retainer separately; press 6 × Ø5×14 pins in radially, 6 × Ø4 coil springs in the step pockets, collar on, retainer 3 × M3 |
| C2_kx74_male_face | PETG | 1.6 | 21 | Snap 3 × Ø10 balls past the equator (1 mm) with a drop of retaining compound; check they can't be pulled out by hand |
| C3_clearance_ladder | PETG | 1.8 | 16 | Print-in-place; try to turn each pin by hand |
| C4_pressfit_ladder | PETG | 4.3 | 64 | Press the real pins/balls in; note the tightest row that still assembles without cracking |
| C5_eccentric_bushing | PETG | 0.2 | 3 | Fit a 6804 bearing; rotate in a Ø38.2 test hole |
| C6_pulleys_20T_40T | PETG | 3.4 | 51 | Check against a real HTD 5M belt: engagement, no tooth ride-over |
| C7_finray_finger | TPU 95A | 0.0 | 0 | Push a Ø66 can into the face: the face should wrap ≥ 30° of the can |
| C8_ratchet_segment | PETG | 3.6 | 21 | Hang 15 kg (×1.2 the moving mass) from a printed pawl on one tooth for 1 h |
| C9_gim_boltcircle | PETG | 1.2 | 12 | Bolt to the GIM6010-8 front and back; read the real PCDs from the ruler ticks |

## Test protocol: fill in the Result column

| # | Measure | Pass if | Updates | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | KX socket: lock twist torque (luggage scale on the collar tab), slip torque (lever + scale), re-seat repeatability over 10 cycles (dial or calipers on a 100 mm arm). Then **creep hold** (loop 29): leave it locked on the P2S bed at 50 °C for 72 h and measure slip torque again | twist ≤ 3 N·m; slip ≥ 12 N·m; repeatability ≤ 0.05 mm at 100 mm; slip loss after the hold ≤ 5 %; preload from the 6 springs 160 ± 30 N (scale on the collar lip) | kx.py: clearance, preload_n, ramp_deg, stroke; rnd/kx_mc.py creep ratio | |
| C2 | Male face: balls seated flush ±0.05 mm | no ball proud > 0.1 mm | kx.py: ball pocket fit | |
| C3 | Smallest gap whose pin turns freely after printing | ≤ 0.30 mm frees | kx.py clearance (0.30) | |
| C4 | Tightest press-fit row that assembles without cracking (pins, balls) | one row works | kx.py pin/ball hole diameters | |
| C5 | Bearing seat fit; eccentric rotates with a lock screw and holds | no play, holds 300 N belt pull | belt_stage.ECC | |
| C6 | Belt engagement on printed pulleys, ride-over under hand load | no ride-over at ~300 N | belt_stage tooth form (DEPTH, GROOVE_R) | |
| C7 | Fin-ray wrap angle on a can; return after 100 squeezes | ≥ 30° wrap; no permanent set | hand.fin_ray (rib count, wall) | |
| C8 | Ratchet tooth under 15 kg for 1 h; pawl engagement | no tooth shear, < 0.5 mm creep | upper.ratchet_strip tooth depth | |
| C9 | Real GIM6010-8 bolt circles (needs one motor) | read PCD to ±0.5 mm | cartridge_m.MOTOR out_pcd / rear_pcd | |
| C10 | D16 wrist-roll dog coupling + spindle (loop 40): 2 × 61804 in the printed skirt; 1 kg on a 90 mm lever, rotate ±180° 1000 times by hand drill at low speed | no play you can feel at the gripper flange; dog slot wear ≤ 0.1 mm | hand.py BRG_WR seats, dog slot width | |
| C11 | Clock-spring ribbon life (loop 40, D14 ribbon in a printed yaw cassette): 10,000 cycles over ±150° on a spare GIM or a drill jig | no conductor break (continuity logged); USB still enumerates at the end | harness.py SPIRAL lengths, ribbon carrier tape | |

**Small parts to buy for the coupons (≈ $25):**
- a pack of Ø5×14 dowel pins
- Ø10 G25 balls
- one 6804-2RS bearing
- a 6–8 coil-spring assortment
- a 300–400 mm HTD 5M-15 belt
- TPU filament if you don't have it

C9 needs one GIM6010-8 (part of the M1 bench-arm kit).

=== FILE: docs/rnd/tip.md ===
# Tip-over: review cases (generated by `python -m rnd.tip`)

Quasi-static zero-moment-point check on the twin's mass model (rnd/twin.py, CAD masses from params):
ZMP = COM_xy − (h_com / g)·a − F_push·h_push / (M g), in the direction of the load, against the x-drive support polygon
(wheel contacts at r = 0.21 m at 0/90/180/270°). The polygon is weakest at 45° between wheels: the edge is only
0.21·cos 45° = 0.148 m from the centre, against 0.21 m toward a wheel.

Cases (both arms straight out at shoulder height, lift mid-travel, holding the load between the hands):
- payload 1 kg (R12) and **2.5 kg two-handed** (box lift)
- load direction toward a wheel (0°) and **between wheels (45°)**
- **braking** from 0.8 m/s in 1.6 s (0.5 m/s², while carrying: the inertial force points the way the robot was going)
- **a human nudge**: 30 N horizontal at 1.0 m (a hand on the shoulder)

Target: ZMP margin ≥ 30 mm in every case (twin v1 dynamic rule); R12 keeps its 50 mm static rule.

**Worst ZMP margin: wheels only -169 mm; with the D18 anti-tip casters -102 mm** (rule ≥ 30 mm). Mass 24.1 kg (+ load), COM height 0.52 m (lift mid, both arms straight out: the worst pose).

**Push to tip over, no load, 1.0 m high:** between wheels 35 N with wheels only, 46 N with the casters (arms tucked, COM centred).

| Load | Direction | Braking 0.5 m/s² | Nudge 30 N @ 1 m | COM offset (mm) | ZMP offset (mm) | Margin, wheels only (mm) | Margin, + D18 casters (mm) | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.0 kg | 0° |  |  | 132 | 132 | 55 | 73 | ✅ |
| 1.0 kg | 0° | yes |  | 132 | 159 | 36 | 48 | ✅ |
| 1.0 kg | 0° |  | yes | 132 | 259 | -35 | -47 | ❌ |
| 1.0 kg | 0° | yes | yes | 132 | 286 | -54 | -71 | ❌ |
| 1.0 kg | 45° |  |  | 132 | 132 | 16 | 79 | ✅ |
| 1.0 kg | 45° | yes |  | 132 | 159 | -10 | 55 | ✅ |
| 1.0 kg | 45° |  | yes | 132 | 259 | -111 | -35 | ❌ |
| 1.0 kg | 45° | yes | yes | 132 | 286 | -137 | -59 | ❌ |
| 2.5 kg | 0° |  |  | 171 | 171 | 27 | 37 | ✅ |
| 2.5 kg | 0° | yes |  | 171 | 198 | 8 | 11 | ❌ |
| 2.5 kg | 0° |  | yes | 171 | 291 | -57 | -76 | ❌ |
| 2.5 kg | 0° | yes | yes | 171 | 318 | -76 | -102 | ❌ |
| 2.5 kg | 45° |  |  | 171 | 171 | -23 | 44 | ✅ |
| 2.5 kg | 45° | yes |  | 171 | 198 | -50 | 20 | ❌ |
| 2.5 kg | 45° |  | yes | 171 | 291 | -142 | -64 | ❌ |
| 2.5 kg | 45° | yes | yes | 171 | 318 | -169 | -88 | ❌ |

**Reading (A10, loop 40):** the x-drive diamond is the weak point, not the mass: its edge is 148 mm from the centre between wheels. **D18:** four spring-loaded ball-transfer casters at the diagonals (≈ 5 N preload, hard stop after 5 mm, so they ride over thresholds) fill the diamond out to an octagon. **Planner rules** for what still fails: with a load over 1 kg, carry it close to the body (elbows bent) and pointing at a wheel (the base is holonomic: a turn costs nothing); cap braking with a load to 0.3 m/s²; slow to a crawl when a person is within reach (a firm shove at shoulder height can tip any 24 kg robot this narrow). 10 of 16 worst-pose cases stay under 30 mm even with the casters; they are covered by the planner rules (no straight-arm carries with a load).

=== FILE: docs/rnd/mc.md ===
# Monte Carlo: dynamic fetch task with imperfections (generated by `python -m rnd.mc`)

Each run draws:
- motor torque derating 0.75–1.0 (all arm, lift and base actuators)
- arm mass ×1.0–1.25 (printed parts heavier than CAD)
- joint friction 0–0.6 N·m (gear and belt drag, the GIM cogging stand-in)
- joint zero-calibration errors σ 0.1° per joint (with joint-side encoders; ESTIMATE)
- finger-object friction μ 0.5–1.2
- can mass 0.2–0.6 kg
- can position error ±15 mm vs the robot's belief

The wrist camera re-measures with σ 3 mm, as designed.
Pass: the can ends upright on the table, with no robot–table contact.

**100/100 runs succeed (100%; 95 % lower bound 96%).** Worst actuator use (of the derated peak): 63%. Min dynamic tip margin 66 mm.

=== FILE: docs/rnd/threshold_mc.md ===
# Threshold / carpet Monte Carlo (generated by `python -m rnd.threshold_mc`)

It reuses rnd/threshold.py's model. Each run draws:
- threshold height U(5, 18) mm: the design limit is 15 mm, so 15–18 mm probes beyond it
- heading 45° ± U(10°): the planner rule D2b, imperfectly executed
- speed U(0.12, 0.18) m/s (rule: ≤ 0.15)
- hard floor or carpet (50/50)
- floor/roller friction μ ×U(0.6, 1.1)
- robot mass 25.6 kg + U(−1, +4): heavier payloads and real parts
- COM height U(0.35, 0.50) m: lift up or down

Pass: it crosses within 1.6× the ideal travel time + 2 s, peak wheel torque ≤ the GIM peak (11 N·m, the actuator limit in the model), and the body
tilts < 15°.
Reported: the success rate by threshold band, and the torque margin (11 / peak) distribution.

**Within the 15 mm design limit: 55/55 cross (100%; 95 % lower bound 93%).** Beyond it (15–18 mm): 25/25.
Torque margin (11 N·m / peak) on successful ≤ 15 mm crossings: min 1.00×, median 1.58×.

=== FILE: docs/rnd/dock_sim.md ===
# Self-docking Monte Carlo (generated by `python -m rnd.dock_sim`)

MuJoCo, planar. The base is a 25 kg disc of R 0.25 m on x/y slides + a yaw hinge, driven by velocity servos whose force is
limited to the wheel peak (approach) or to a soft limit (final push).

The dock is a printed V block. Its two faces are at ±DOCK_V_DEG to the dock axis, with the spring contacts on the
centre line, and it is screwed to the floor. The robot's contact pads sit on the skirt at azimuth PAD_AZ (between two
wheels, in view of the head camera).

Each run draws:
- a start pose ±0.3 m / ±20° around a point 1.0 m out
- a dock-pose estimate error: σ 10 mm lateral, σ 1.5° (lidar profile + AprilTag; ESTIMATE)
- wheel slip: speed scale U(0.9, 1.1) and a lateral drift velocity σ 10 mm/s

Approach:
1. Go to a pre-dock point 0.35 m out along the *estimated* axis, with the pads facing the dock.
2. Drive in at 0.10 m/s (60 N push limit) with a yaw hold. A weak lateral hold applies until first contact; after that the
   lateral axis is current-limited to 5 N, so the V faces centre the disc.
3. Stop when both faces push back (> 15 N each), or after 3 s in contact.

Pass: the pad centre lies within ±CAPTURE of the dock contacts laterally, yaw error ≤ 5°, and the disc is pressed into
the V with its centre within 8 mm of the dock axis.

**100/100 dock successfully (100%; 95 % lower bound 96%).**
Pad lateral error: mean 4.7 mm, worst 18.8 mm (capture ±25). Yaw error: worst 4.4°. Seated: 100/100.

=== FILE: docs/rnd/twin.md ===
# Simulation twin: reach and tip-over (auto-generated by `python -m rnd.run_all`)

Model generated from `rnd/params.py`: arm 0.3+0.28+0.156 m, wrist ±120°, shoulder height 0.35–1.05 m, payload 1.0 kg in the right hand, left arm tucked. Reach = IK (lift + 6 joints) to within 1 cm and 20° of the required gripper direction. Grid: x = 0.30–0.65 m from the robot centre (base edge at 0.25 m), y across the right arm's side.

| Target | Reach coverage | Tip margin per layout (mm / max base accel m/s²) |
|---|---|---|
| counter, angled 45° grasp (z 0.95) | 100% | X wheel-fwd R.21 (chosen): 73 / 1.3; X edge-fwd R.21: 60 / 1.0; kiwi R.21 (rejected): 44 / 0.8 |
| floor pickup (z 0.03, gripper down) | 100% | X wheel-fwd R.21 (chosen): 77 / 2.0; X edge-fwd R.21: 60 / 1.6; kiwi R.21 (rejected): 47 / 1.2 |
| dishwasher low rack (z 0.20, down) | 94% | X wheel-fwd R.21 (chosen): 64 / 1.5; X edge-fwd R.21: 48 / 1.1; kiwi R.21 (rejected): 38 / 0.9 |
| table top (z 0.78, down) | 95% | X wheel-fwd R.21 (chosen): 69 / 1.1; X edge-fwd R.21: 57 / 0.9; kiwi R.21 (rejected): 41 / 0.7 |
| counter top-down only (z 0.95) [info: counters use top-down OR 45°, see D4b] | 52% | X wheel-fwd R.21 (chosen): 69 / 1.1; X edge-fwd R.21: 57 / 0.9; kiwi R.21 (rejected): 41 / 0.6 |
| shelf (z 1.20, horizontal) | 100% | X wheel-fwd R.21 (chosen): 85 / 1.4; X edge-fwd R.21: 70 / 1.1; kiwi R.21 (rejected): 53 / 0.8 |
| high shelf (z 1.40, horizontal) | 100% | X wheel-fwd R.21 (chosen): 88 / 1.3; X edge-fwd R.21: 80 / 1.1; kiwi R.21 (rejected): 55 / 0.8 |

## Two-handed tasks (shared lift, both arms, collision-free)

| Task | Feasible |
|---|---|
| hold a box at the table (hands 24 cm apart, z 0.85) | ✅ |
| hand-to-hand at chest height (handover) | ✅ |
| lift a box from the floor (hands 30 cm apart, z 0.10) | ✅ |
| fold a towel on the table (hands 40 cm apart, down, z 0.80) | ✅ |

=== FILE: docs/BUILD_v4.md ===
# Pip v4: build guide (generated by `python -m rnd.build_guide`)

**First build:** about **23 h hands-on** (ESTIMATE), plus about **326 h of unattended P2S printing** (13.9 kg PETG). Reconfiguring later is fast: a cartridge swap is one collar twist (~1 min), and an arm comes off with one V-band clamp.
**One arm** (the first milestone build): about **6.0 h** hands-on.

Parts: [BOM](rnd/BOM_v4.md) · prints: [print plan](rnd/print_plan.md) · wiring: [electrical](rnd/electrical.md) · checks: [assembly](rnd/assembly.md), [sim](rnd/sim.md)

| Step | What | Count | Min each | Hours |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Print | Print the parts in print_plan.md order: cartridges + KX parts first (they let you bench-test motors early) | 1 | 0 | 0.0 |
| 2 Coupons | Print + test the KX74 coupon set and the GIM bolt-circle coupon; adjust KX fit / bolt circles in rnd/parts before mass printing | 1 | 45 | 0.8 |
| 3 KX sockets | D15 (loop 40): press 6 dowel pins in radially, 6 coil springs into the step pockets, collar on, retainer (3 × M3 into inserts); snap 3 balls into the male face with retaining compound | 13 | 8 | 1.7 |
| 4 Cartridges | Motor into its can (3 screws), torque pin, adapter (4 screws, balls embedded at a print pause); label + CAN ID | 21 | 6 | 2.1 |
| 5 Belt stages | D13 (loop 40): flange coupling onto the KX hub (4 × M4); 6801s into the eccentric bushings; Ø12 shaft through wall A, aluminium pulley on, wall B; split big pulley between the walls, Ø40 tube through; belt on, tension with the bushings | 9 | 14 | 2.1 |
| 5b Clock springs | Wind the 6 ribbon cassettes per arm (D14 ribbon) and fit them at pitch, roll, yaw, elbow, wrist pitch, wrist roll | 2 | 30 | 1.0 |
| 6 Arms | Bond the CF tubes into the sockets (epoxy, cure overnight); upper bone + elbow stage, forearm + wrist can, hand + gripper, roll/yaw module | 2 | 90 | 3.0 |
| 7 Shoulders | Pitch modules: bracket, shaft, balancer (spring cage, cord, pulley A at the 50 mm slot), V-band flange | 2 | 45 | 1.5 |
| 8 Mast + torso + head | Ratchet strip segments, lift drive + belt, carriage (8 × 625), torso halves around the carriage, head bracket + pan/tilt + shell | 1 | 150 | 2.5 |
| 9 Base | 4 wheel modules (pods, shafts, hubs, wheels), deck quarters, skirt, coupling, battery bay, lidar, electronics | 1 | 120 | 2.0 |
| 10 Wiring | Pre-made daisy-chain harness segments (JST-GH CAN/TTL, VH power) per module; power distribution board; detach plugs | 1 | 120 | 2.0 |
| 10b Sensors (D9) | Joint-side encoder magnets + boards (9), fingertip tactile pads (4), palm ToF (2), cliff ToF (4), 3 RP2040 CAN sensor nodes | 1 | 160 | 2.7 |
| 11 Bring-up | Per-cartridge test on the bench, joint zeroing (magnet index), balancer K tune, lift pawl test, e-stop + regen clamp + watchdog test | 1 | 120 | 2.0 |
| | **Total hands-on** | | | **23.4** |

**Against R10** (first build ≤ 4 h): **not met.**
- **Loop 10 cut the estimate from 25 h to ~19 h:**
    - print-in-place KX sockets with pins and spring embedded at print pauses (no retainer, no screws) — *retired in loop 40 (D15): the screwed socket takes ≈ 8 min*
    - 7 instead of 14 screws per cartridge
    - eccentric-bushing belt tensioning
    - pre-made harness segments
- **A 21-actuator robot won't reach 4 h.** Precedent: a 6-servo LeRobot SO-101 arm takes a few hours.
- **Proposed target split (owner decision):** first build of one arm ≤ 4 h; whole robot ≤ 15 h; any module swap ≤ 5 min.

## Safety notes
- 24 V Li-ion: use the specified BMS (≥ 30 A), fuse every branch, and charge on a non-flammable surface.
- The arms can pinch. Torque limits are set in firmware (rated × 1.8). The KX seats also slip at about 1.16× actuator peak as a mechanical backstop.
- Test the lift pawl and the e-stop before any powered arm motion.

=== FILE: rnd/params.py ===
```python
"""Single source of truth for Pip v4 design parameters (SI units: m, kg, N·m, s).

Every analysis (budget, arm sizing, drivetrain, twin) and, later, the CAD reads
these values. Change a number here and re-run `python -m rnd.run_all`.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from copy import deepcopy
from dataclasses import dataclass, field, asdict
from pathlib import Path

import yaml

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
SPECS = yaml.safe_load((ROOT / "specs" / "actuators.yaml").read_text())
COMP = yaml.safe_load((ROOT / "specs" / "components.yaml").read_text())
G = 9.81


def act(name: str) -> dict:
    """Actuator spec in SI: mass kg, rated/peak N·m, speed rad/s."""
    s = SPECS[name]
    rpm = s.get("rated_speed_rpm") or 45
    return {
        "mass": s["mass_g"] / 1000 if not isinstance(s["mass_g"], list) else sum(s["mass_g"]) / 2000,
        "rated": s.get("rated_torque_nm"),
        "peak": s.get("peak_torque_nm") or s.get("stall_torque_nm"),
        "speed": rpm * 2 * 3.14159 / 60,
        "price": sum(s["price_usd"]) / 2,
    }


@dataclass
class Arm:
    l_upper: float = 0.30          # shoulder pitch axis -> elbow axis (reach sweep: 0.25 -> 0.30)
    l_fore: float = 0.28           # elbow -> wrist pitch axis (reach sweep: 0.23 -> 0.28)
    l_hand: float = 0.156          # wrist pitch -> grasp point (loop 10 hand CAD: wrist roll + KX44 + gripper servo stack; was 0.13; loop 40 D16 wrist-roll spindle: +6 mm)
    shoulder_y: float = 0.20       # |y| of shoulder pitch axis from robot centre line
    shoulder_x: float = 0.06       # x of shoulder axis relative to spine
    roll_offset_y: float = 0.083   # roll axis outboard of the pitch body / detach plane (loop 10 roll CAD: 0.05 → 0.075, the 72T pulley; loop 40: 0.083, the 80T pulley)
    yaw_drop: float = 0.1134       # roll axis → upper-arm yaw interface (loop 10 roll CAD: 0.07 → 0.10; loop 40 D13 yaw stage + 80T roll pulley: 0.113 = roll.YAW_DROP); the upper bone shortens to keep l_upper
    # actuator choice per joint and belt/linkage reduction in the bone
    joints: dict = field(default_factory=lambda: {
        "shoulder_pitch": ("GIM6010-8", 3.0),   # loop 10: 2.5 → 3 (20T:60T) after the hand CAD added 0.2 kg at the tip
        "shoulder_roll":  ("GIM6010-8", 80 / 18),   # loop 40: 4.0 → 4.44 (18T:80T, D13 mass); loop 10: 3.5 → 4 (18T:72T); loop 5: 3 → 3.5
        "upper_yaw":      ("GIM6010-8", 2.0),   # loop 5: DM-J4310 direct was under-rated over the workspace (p95 4.6 N·m vs 3 rated)
        "elbow":          ("GIM6010-8", 2.0),
        "wrist_pitch":    ("DM-J4310-2EC_V1.1", 1.0),
        "wrist_roll":     ("STS3215-C018", 1.0),
        "gripper":        ("STS3215-C018", 1.0),
    })
    spring_k_nm: float = 4.5       # shoulder balancer torque at the horizontal arm, τ = K·sin(pitch). Loop 5 (rnd/spring.py):
                                   # replaces the "85 % everywhere" assumption; K tuned for a 0.5 kg typical payload
    bone_mass_upper: float = 0.75  # loop 40: +98 g housing stiffener (A8, P2 FEA; rnd/parts/arm.py housing_stiffener). FROM CAD (loop 23 roll-up: shell + cap + torque arm 216 g, cassette 52 g, CF neck 10 g, 40T hub 92 g, elbow small hub + KX74 108 g, bearings 90 g, hardware 70 g ESTIMATE = 0.638 kg; +9 g for the 110 mm housing = 0.647), rounded up
    bone_mass_fore: float = 0.31   # FROM CAD (loop 9): side link with CF Ø30 tube + wrist KX56 socket + bearings + fasteners
    bone_wall_mm: float = 1.2      # printed shell wall (rnd/bones.py: strength is never binding)
    cf_tubes: bool = True          # carbon-tube neck + forearm (loop 9: bone-bending sag at the hand 4.1 → 1.5 mm)
    gripper_mech_mass: float = 0.30  # FROM CAD (loop 16 gripper v2: fin-ray TPU fingers, tactile pads, palm ToF; rnd/parts/hand.py)
    wrist_cam_mass: float = 0.05
    payload: float = 1.0           # kg at the grasp point (R2)
    # Elbow motor placement: None = at the elbow; a distance (m from the shoulder pitch axis) = motor sits up the
    # upper arm, just past the yaw joint, and drives the elbow through a long belt (precedent: Berkeley Blue moves
    # motors back along belts). The elbow then carries only a pulley hub + bearings.
    elbow_motor_at: float | None = None
    pitch_module_mass: float = 0.79    # FROM CAD (loop 7; loop 8 lightening 0.81 → 0.76; loop 10 60T pulley 0.79, rnd/parts/shoulder.py): bracket, pitch shaft + 50T pulley + balancer lever +
                                       # KX-V ferrule, small hub + KX74 socket, bearings, spring/cable/pulley A, V-band clamp. Torso side.
    roll_stage_mass: float = 0.71      # FROM CAD (loop 40, rnd/parts/roll.py masses(split=True)): ferrule, roll link + spring ring, roll shaft + split 72T + spring hub, D13 stage + KX74 socket, 2×61808. Loop 10: 0.58
    yaw_stage_mass: float = 0.56       # FROM CAD (loop 40, incl. the A8 box walls): clevis + yaw block, yaw shaft + flange + split 40T + spring ring, D13 stage + KX74 socket, 2×61808. Loop 10: 0.47
    elbow_hub_mass: float = 0.12   # big pulley + hub + 2×61808 + tensioner when the motor is remote (ESTIMATE)
    wrist_pitch_range_deg: float = 120.0   # ±140 folded the hand into the forearm (collision study); ±120 is realistic and reaches more


@dataclass
class Base:
    layout: str = "xdrive"         # xdrive (4 omni) — kiwi tip margin was only ~50 mm (twin v0)
    radius: float = 0.25           # shell radius
    wheel_radius_pos: float = 0.21 # wheel contact points from centre
    wheel_d: float = 0.1524        # 6" omni
    wheel_motor: str = "GIM6010-8"
    clearance: float = 0.025
    height: float = 0.13
    struct_mass: float = 2.0       # FROM CAD (build-out, rnd/parts/base.py): pods, wheel shafts, KX74 wheel hubs, ribbed deck, cans, 8×61808, screws (was 1.4)
    shell_mass: float = 0.33       # FROM CAD: four skirt quarters
    electronics_mass: float = 0.35 # DC-DC, CAN, flow sensor, wiring, e-stop
    lidar_mass: float = 0.15
    coupling_mass: float = 0.25    # upper/base coupling + cam lever + connector
    battery_mass: float = 1.10     # loop 13: Lumenier NAV-class 6S 10 Ah 21700 pack 975 g + 30 A BMS + printed sleeve (was 1.5 ESTIMATE)
    battery_wh: float = 222.0


@dataclass
class Upper:
    mast_w: float = 0.060          # square mast tube width
    lift_min: float = 0.35         # shoulder height (z) at lowest (reach sweep)
    lift_max: float = 1.05         # reach sweep: 0.95 -> 1.05
    spine_len: float = 1.06        # extrusion length
    spine_mass_per_m: float = 1.25 # loop 10 (rnd/mast.py): 60×60×2 Al square tube, torso sway 1.4 mm (2040 swayed 5.5 mm)
    lift_motor_mass: float = 0.81  # FROM CAD (build-out, rnd/parts/upper.py): GIM cartridge + 20T + mount + 2.4 m belt + idler + ratchet strip
    lift_motor: str = "GIM6010-8"
    carriage_mass: float = 0.58    # FROM CAD: printed sleeve + 8×625 bearings + pawl + solenoid
    torso_struct_mass: float = 0.867 # FROM CAD: rounded two-piece body enclosing the pitch modules (looks pass) + electronics tray
    compute_mass: float = 0.45     # Pi 5 + Jetson option + CAN + DC-DC
    head_mass: float = 0.94        # FROM CAD (looks pass) 0.82 + D11 (loop 28): 4.3" screen board ~90 g (ESTIMATE), shell +16 mm ~25 g, larger bezel ~8 g, − eye LEDs
    head_on_mast: bool = True      # owner suggestion (2026-11-25): head fixed on top of the spine; torso + arms ride the lift below it
    cable_mass: float = 0.25
    handle_mass: float = 0.0       # now part of the head bracket (FROM CAD)
    # Carry split: each arm detaches at its shoulder-pitch KX socket (one collar twist + a blind-mate plug).
    # The pitch cartridge, its stage and the spring stay on the torso; everything outboard is the "arm" piece.
    arms_detach: bool = True


@dataclass
class Design:
    arm: Arm = field(default_factory=Arm)
    base: Base = field(default_factory=Base)
    upper: Upper = field(default_factory=Upper)

    def copy(self, **changes) -> "Design":
        d = deepcopy(self)
        for k, v in changes.items():
            obj, attr = k.split(".")
            setattr(getattr(d, obj), attr, v)
        return d

    def to_dict(self):
        return asdict(self)


BASELINE = Design()

```

```yaml
# Pip v4 actuator specs (T01). Every value has a source; ESTIMATE = unverified.
# Prices are retailer snapshots (2026-11), shipping/tax extra.
GIM6010-8:
  role: cartridge M (shoulders, elbows, base wheels)
  maker: SteadyWin
  gear_ratio: 8
  voltage_nominal_v: 24
  voltage_range_v: "12-28 per manual; some retailers list 12-56 (driver variant). DESIGN TO <=28 V"
  rated_torque_nm: 5
  peak_torque_nm: 11
  rated_speed_rpm: 120     # after reduction, ±10 %
  max_speed_rpm: 420       # after reduction, ±10 %
  mass_g: 388              # with SDC104 driver (370 g without)
  outer_diameter_mm: 80    # retailer description; confirm with installation drawing (ESTIMATE until drawing checked)
  interface: "CAN + USB-C (SDC104 driver), integrated driver + encoder"
  continuous_current_a: 10.5
  price_usd: [90, 94]      # loop 14 verified with driver: aifitlab $90 (GDS68), SteadyWin store $93.88; bare motor $70.35
  notes: >
    Some Amazon variants differ (48 V/4.6 N·m/17.9 N·m locking). Buy the 24 V SDC104 version and verify on arrival.
  sources:
    - https://sdalal1.github.io/assets/pdf/SteadyWin_GIM6010-8_Manual_rev1.pdf
    - https://actuatorlist.com/actuators/steadywin-gim6010-8
    - https://www.roboticscenter.ai/store/product/steadywin-gim6010-8-planetary-reducer-servo-motor/

DM-J4310-2EC_V1.1:

  interface: "CAN 1 Mbps (MIT/position/speed modes), UART config, dual encoder (output-absolute)"

  price_usd: [125, 179]

DM-J4340P-2EC:

    - https://rcdrone.top/products/damiao-dm-j8009p-2ec-servo-motor
```

=== FILE: rnd/parts/arm.py ===
```python
"""Arm bones v0: upper arm and forearm shells built around the KX sockets and belt stages.

Layout (upper-arm frame: origin at the yaw interface, arm hanging along -Z, elbow axis along Y at z = -ELBOW_Z):
- Upper bone: flange (bolts to the yaw hub) → Ø72 neck tube → elbow housing that holds the elbow cartridge M
  (axis Y, 70 mm above the elbow axis), its pulley hub (2 × 61804), the belt, and the 40T elbow pulley on 2 × 61808.
- Forearm: a **side link** bolted to the elbow pulley hub on the +Y side. Its distal housing holds the wrist-pitch
  cartridge with the output facing **back toward the arm's centre plane** (-Y). The hand therefore sits close to the
  centre plane (net lateral offset ≈ 0), and it swings *beside* the forearm link instead of folding into it.
Walls are 1.6 mm (4 perimeters at 0.4 mm), printed solid. Every part fits in 256 mm when split as noted in `PRINT`.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from build123d import Align, Axis, Box, Cylinder, Part, Pos, Rot, fillet

from . import cartridge_m as cm
from .soft import soft_shell
from .kx import KX56, KX74, hub as kx_hub, collar as kx_collar

MIN = (Align.CENTER, Align.CENTER, Align.MIN)
WALL = 1.2                  # 3 perimeters at 0.4 mm. Loop 9 (rnd/bones.py): strength is never binding; stiffness comes from the CF tubes
from ..params import BASELINE as _D
ELBOW_Z = (_D.arm.l_upper - _D.arm.yaw_drop) * 1000   # yaw interface → elbow axis (loop 10: 230 → 200 mm)
FORE_L = 280.0              # elbow axis → wrist axis (params: l_fore)
BELT_C = 70.0               # elbow belt centre distance (rnd/belts.py)
HOUSING = dict(x=110.0, y=108.0, z0=-ELBOW_Z - 42.0, z1=-ELBOW_Z + 70.0 + 56.0, rv=34.0, rv_minus=14.0)   # loop 23: 98 → 110 wide, +8 top (the elbow KX collar lugs need r 52.5 clear through its 35° twist); corner r 14 on the −Y half, 34 on the +Y (forearm) half
J4310 = dict(od=57.0, h=46.0)   # DM-J4310 (actuators.yaml)
FORE_Y = HOUSING["y"] / 2 + 18.0  # forearm link centre plane (+Y side link); loop 23: +6 so the CF socket/tube clear the housing over the full elbow range
# elbow belt cassette (housing frame, y spans of the two plates): set by the D13 small stage v2 bearings (loop 38), whose
# positions are fixed by the cartridge stack (KX face at y = -20, stack toward +Y: y = -20 - z_stage).
from .belt_stage import V2 as _V2
_y = lambda z: -20.0 - z
BELT_Y = _y(_V2["belt_c"])                                         # 20.2
CAS_A = (_y(_V2["jA"][1]) - 1.0, _y(_V2["jA"][0]))                  # 6 mm plate, bearing A flush on the pulley side (3.7 … 9.7; 0.5 mm to the pulley)
CAS_B = (_y(_V2["jB"][1]), _y(_V2["jB"][0]) + 0.2)                  # bearing B flush on the pulley side; loop 40: 6 → 5.2 mm (the D15 hub moved it 0.8 mm toward the +Y corner skin)
CAS_POST_X, CAS_POST_Z = 35.0, -ELBOW_Z + BELT_C - 10.0     # loop 38: 32 → 35, boss r 7 (R15: belt ≥ 3 mm, +Y corner ≥ 1.5 mm)
CAP_Z = HOUSING["z0"] + 10.0          # housing / bottom-cap split
WRIST_Y0, WRIST_Y1 = 43.0, 96.0   # wrist can span (holds the DM-J4310 cartridge; its KX56 face at y ≈ 33.6, hand centre at y = 0)


def _rbox(x, y, z, r, align=(Align.CENTER, Align.CENTER, Align.CENTER)):
    b = Box(x, y, z, align=align)
    return fillet(b.edges().filter_by(Axis.Z), min(r, x / 2 - 0.1, y / 2 - 0.1))


def _housing_solid(inset=0.0):
    """Loop 23: housing envelope with big soft corners on the +Y half (the forearm's CF tube and socket swing within
    3 mm of it) and tighter corners on the −Y half (room for the KX collar Ø98 and the cartridge can)."""
    from .soft import soft_box
    h = HOUSING
    hz = h["z1"] - h["z0"]
    c = Pos(0, 0, (h["z0"] + h["z1"]) / 2)
    i = inset
    plus = c * soft_box(h["x"] - 2 * i, h["y"] - 2 * i, hz - 2 * i, max(h["rv"] - i, 1), max(26 - i, 0.6))
    minus = c * soft_box(h["x"] - 2 * i, h["y"] - 2 * i, hz - 2 * i, max(h["rv_minus"] - i, 1), max(26 - i, 0.6))
    return (plus & Pos(0, 100, 0) * Box(400, 200, 600)) + (minus & Pos(0, -100, 0) * Box(400, 200, 600))


def housing_shell():
    return _housing_solid(0.0) - _housing_solid(WALL)


STIFF_T = 2.8                # loop 40 (A8): inner layer on the +Y half and the top (1.2 + 2.8 = 4 mm)


def housing_stiffener() -> Part:
    """Loop 40 (A8, rnd/stiffness.py): with P2 elements the 1.2 mm housing tilted the elbow bosses ≈ 39 mrad under 1 kg
    straight out (≈ 20 mm at the hand; P1 had shown 5 mrad). The forearm load enters the +Y boss and has to reach the
    neck socket on top: the +Y half and the top become 4 mm (an inner 2.8 mm layer), and four 3 mm gussets tie the neck
    socket to the top plate. The −Y half (cartridge bay, KX collar room) keeps its 1.2 mm skin."""
    h = HOUSING
    inner = _housing_solid(WALL - 0.4) - _housing_solid(WALL + STIFF_T)          # 0.4 mm overlap into the skin: coincident faces crash the fuse
    yw = h["y"] / 2 - WALL - STIFF_T - 0.5                                               # +Y face slab only (cassette plate B ends at y 43.7)
    st = (inner & Pos(0, yw + 100, 0) * Box(400, 200, 600)) + (inner & Pos(0, 0, h["z1"] - 10) * Box(400, 400, 20))
    st -= Pos(0, -20, -ELBOW_Z + BELT_C) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * Cylinder(61.0, 40)          # keep the elbow KX collar and its thumb tabs (r ≈ 57) clear through the twist (61: meshes cleanly)
    for k in range(4):
        a = 45 + 90 * k
        st += Rot(0, 0, a) * Pos(23 + 14, 0, h["z1"]) * Box(28, 3, 14, align=(Align.CENTER, Align.CENTER, Align.MIN))
    return st


def _hollow_rbox(x, y, z, r, wall=WALL):
    return _rbox(x, y, z, r) - _rbox(x - 2 * wall, y - 2 * wall, z - 2 * wall, max(r - wall, 0.5))


def _ring(od, idd, t):
    return Cylinder(od / 2, t) - Cylinder(idd / 2, t)


def upper_bone() -> Part:
    h = HOUSING
    p = Pos(0, 0, -6) * Cylinder(45, 6, align=MIN)                                    # yaw-hub flange
    # neck (loop 9): carbon tube Ø40×1 (see cf_neck()) bonded into two printed sockets, one on the flange and one on the housing
    for z in (-6 - 20, h["z1"] - 2):
        p += Pos(0, 0, z) * (Cylinder(23, 22, align=MIN) - Cylinder(20.1, 22, align=MIN))
    hz = h["z1"] - h["z0"]
    p += housing_shell()                                                                 # looks pass: soft on every edge (loop 23: two corner radii)
    p += housing_stiffener()                                                             # loop 40 (A8): P2 FEA found the 1.2 mm shell far too soft
    # bearing bosses through the ±Y walls: elbow axis (61808, OD 52). The small-pulley hub runs in the elbow belt
    # cassette (confidence campaign: FEA showed the shell can't carry belt tension; the journals never reached the walls)
    p += Pos(0, -h["y"] / 2, -ELBOW_Z) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * _ring(52.0 + 10, 52.0, 8)
    p += Pos(0, h["y"] / 2 - 3.5, -ELBOW_Z) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * _ring(52.0 + 10, 52.0, 7)   # loop 23: +Y boss inside the wall (the forearm disc runs 1 mm outside it)
    p += elbow_torque_arm()
    # loop 23: window for the elbow KX collar's twist tab (its ±36° sweep pokes through the wall; also the access to lock it)
    #    tab at r ≤ 57 on +X, sweeping 0 → +35° (locked → unlocked) about the cartridge axis: one clean rectangular window in the +X wall
    p -= Pos(49.0, -20.5, -ELBOW_Z + BELT_C) * Box(24, 23, 94)
    # anti-rotation slot block for the cassette pin (slot radial to the elbow axis, so belt tension never reaches the shell)
    p += Pos(0, (CAS_B[1] + 2 + h["y"] / 2 - WALL) / 2, -92) * (Box(16, h["y"] / 2 - WALL - CAS_B[1] - 2, 16)
                                                                - Box(5.4, 40, 12))
    # openings: cartridge bay on -Y (the cartridge drops in from the side), elbow hub exit on +Y
    p -= Pos(0, -h["y"] / 2, -ELBOW_Z + BELT_C) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * Cylinder(cm.MOTOR["od"] / 2 + 4.5, 20)   # loop 36 (R15): 1.2 mm around the can
    p -= Pos(0, h["y"] / 2, -ELBOW_Z) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * Cylinder(26, 20)
    p -= Pos(0, -h["y"] / 2 + 3, -ELBOW_Z) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * Cylinder(26, 20, align=MIN)   # loop 40: −Y wall opened for the elbow shaft (was blind; the shaft now goes in from −Y)
    # (the old torque-pin slot cut here sat inside the bay hole and removed nothing: the cartridge torque-reaction path is
    #  open on every stage — see rnd/integration.py and the RND_PLAN queue)
    # bottom cap is a separate print (bone_cap()): the cassette goes in from below
    return p - Pos(0, 0, CAP_Z) * Box(200, 200, 100, align=(Align.CENTER, Align.CENTER, Align.MAX))


def elbow_torque_arm() -> Part:
    """Loop 23: torque reaction for the elbow cartridge. The can is clocked 180° (assembly) so its pin points up:
    pin at (x 0, z = zS + 30) behind the can rear (y = -20 - zc). Two 14 × 6 mm rails, fused on the housing top
    beside the CF neck socket, run over the can to a 7 mm fork plate. Its slot is open downward (toward the cartridge
    axis). In-plane bending ≈ 6.7 MPa at 367 N (hand calc)."""
    h = HOUSING
    tp = cm.TORQUE_PIN
    zc = KX74.gap + KX74.face_t + cm.NECK + cm.FLOOR + cm.MOTOR["h"]
    zS = -ELBOW_Z + BELT_C
    y_rear, zp = -20 - zc, zS + tp["r"]
    z_top = h["z1"]
    root = -26.0                                                     # clear of the CF neck socket (r 23)
    a = None
    for x in (-23.0, 23.0):                                                                     # two 14 × 6 rails (≈ 6.7 MPa at 367 N)
        r = Pos(x, (root + y_rear - 9) / 2, z_top + 3) * Box(14, root - (y_rear - 9), 6)
        r += Pos(x, root - 7, z_top - 4) * Box(14, 14, 14)                                      # root pad fused through the top skin
        a = r if a is None else a + r
    f = Pos(0, y_rear - 5.5, (zp - 9 + z_top + 6) / 2) * Box(48, 7, z_top + 6 - (zp - 9))
    f -= Pos(0, y_rear - 5.5, zp - 9 + 7) * Box(tp["d"] + 0.4, 8, 14 + 0.01)       # open bottom, closed 5 mm above the pin
    return a + f - _housing_solid(WALL) - Pos(0, -h["y"] / 2, zS) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * Cylinder(cm.MOTOR["od"] / 2 + 4, 30)   # keep the bay hole clear


def bone_cap() -> Part:
    """Screw-on bottom cap of the elbow housing (4 × M3 into heat-set inserts in the corners, ESTIMATE) with a 5 mm
    locating spigot. It closes the opening the elbow belt cassette is inserted through."""
    h = HOUSING
    hz = h["z1"] - h["z0"]
    full = housing_shell()
    cap = full & Pos(0, 0, CAP_Z) * Box(200, 200, 100, align=(Align.CENTER, Align.CENTER, Align.MAX))
    lx, ly = h["x"] - 2 * WALL - 12.6, h["y"] - 2 * WALL - 12.6                           # clears the soft bottom edge (r 26)
    lip = _rbox(lx, ly, 5, 26.5) - _rbox(lx - 3.2, ly - 3.2, 5, 24.9)                     # open 1.6 mm ring
    return cap + Pos(0, 0, CAP_Z + 2.5) * lip


def cassette_plate(side: str = "B") -> Part:
    """Elbow belt cassette (confidence campaign): one of two 7 mm plates that carry the small-pulley hub (61804 in an
    eccentric bushing, Ø38.2 seat) and ride on the elbow pulley hub on their own thin 6708 (40×50×6, Ø50 seat;
    ~11 g vs 26 g for a 61808, which keeps the shoulder-roll workspace margin ≥ 1.5×; C0 ≈ 2.4 kN vs 0.7 kN belt peak). The belt loop closes
    inside the cassette. Plate B has the anti-rotation pin tab. Housing frame."""
    from build123d import Sketch, Circle, Rectangle, make_hull, extrude, Plane, Locations
    y0, y1 = CAS_A if side == "A" else CAS_B
    zE, zS = -ELBOW_Z, -ELBOW_Z + BELT_C
    t = y1 - y0
    disc = lambda x, z, r: Pos(x, y0, z) * Rot(-90, 0, 0) * Cylinder(r, t, align=MIN)
    p = disc(0, zE, 31) + disc(0, zS, 27)
    # web between the seats (hull approximated by a tapered box)
    p += Pos(0, y0 + t / 2, (zE + zS) / 2) * Box(54, t, BELT_C)
    for x in (-CAS_POST_X, CAS_POST_X):
        p += disc(x, CAS_POST_Z, 7.0) + Pos(x / 2, y0 + t / 2, CAS_POST_Z) * Box(abs(x), t, 14)
        p -= disc(x, CAS_POST_Z, 2.75)                                    # M5 through-bolt
    if side == "B":
        p += Pos(0, y0 + t / 2, (zS + 27 - 12 + -92) / 2) * Box(16, t, (-92) - (zS + 27 - 12) + 6)
    p -= disc(0, zE, 25) + disc(0, zS, 19.1)                              # 6708 (40×50×6) seat; eccentric-bushing seat
    return p


def cassette_spacer() -> Part:
    """Spacer tube between the cassette plates (2 per cassette), Ø14 over an M5 bolt."""
    return Pos(0, CAS_A[1], CAS_POST_Z) * Rot(-90, 0, 0) * (Cylinder(7, CAS_B[0] - CAS_A[1], align=MIN)
                                                            - Cylinder(2.75, CAS_B[0] - CAS_A[1], align=MIN))


def cassette() -> Part:
    return cassette_plate("A") + cassette_plate("B") + sum((Pos(x, 0, 0) * cassette_spacer() for x in (-CAS_POST_X, CAS_POST_X)), Part())


def forearm() -> Part:
    """Forearm frame: origin on the elbow axis at the upper arm's centre plane; arm hangs along -Z."""
    y = FORE_Y
    link_t = 22.0
    p = Pos(0, y, 0) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * Cylinder(40, link_t)                            # hub disc on the elbow pulley hub
    p -= Pos(0, y, 0) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * Cylinder(21, link_t)
    body_len = FORE_L - 40 - 38
    # link (loop 9): carbon tube Ø30×1 (see cf_fore()) bonded into a socket on the hub disc and one on the wrist can
    for z0 in (-40 - 24, -FORE_L + 38):
        p += Pos(0, y, z0) * (Cylinder(18, 24, align=MIN) - Cylinder(15.1, 24, align=MIN))
    # wrist housing: holds the wrist cartridge (axis Y, output toward -Y)
    # (loop 10) sized to the real DM-J4310 cartridge (rnd/parts/hand.py): KX56 face at y = WRIST_Y0, open toward -Y
    can_d = J4310["od"] + 2 * 2.4 + 0.6 + 2.0               # loop 36 (R15): 1 mm radial float for the cartridge (was 0.3)
    wy = WRIST_Y1 - WRIST_Y0
    p += Pos(0, WRIST_Y0, -FORE_L) * Rot(-90, 0, 0) * (Cylinder(can_d / 2 + WALL, wy, align=MIN) - Cylinder(can_d / 2, wy - WALL, align=MIN))
    p += Pos(0, y, -FORE_L + 38) * Box(56, link_t, 12)                                  # web joining link and wrist can
    # loop 24: torque reaction for the wrist-pitch cartridge D. Its Ø5 pin (r 22, +X) sticks out of the can rear through
    # the 1.2 mm end wall; a 7.2 mm pad outside the wall, fused to the can rim, carries a radial slot (DM-J4310 peak
    # 7 N·m / r 22 = 318 N → ≈ 8.8 MPa bearing on 5 × 7.2 mm).
    pad_y0, pad_y1 = WRIST_Y1 - WALL, WRIST_Y1 + 6.0
    p += Pos((12 + can_d / 2 + WALL) / 2, (pad_y0 + pad_y1) / 2, -FORE_L) * Box(can_d / 2 + WALL - 12, pad_y1 - pad_y0, 16)
    p -= Pos((14 + 26.5) / 2, (pad_y0 + pad_y1) / 2, -FORE_L) * Box(26.5 - 14, pad_y1 - pad_y0 + 2, 5.4)
    return p


def cf_neck() -> Part:
    """Carbon tube Ø40×1, flange socket → housing socket."""
    z_top, z_bot = -6.0, HOUSING["z1"] + 20
    return Pos(0, 0, z_bot) * (Cylinder(20, z_top - z_bot, align=MIN) - Cylinder(19, z_top - z_bot, align=MIN))


def cf_fore() -> Part:
    """Carbon tube Ø30×1 along the forearm side link."""
    z_top, z_bot = -40.0, -FORE_L + 38 + 24
    return Pos(0, FORE_Y, z_bot) * (Cylinder(15, z_top - z_bot, align=MIN) - Cylinder(14, z_top - z_bot, align=MIN))


def elbow_pulley_hub() -> Part:
    """Elbow shaft: Ø40 tube (2 × 61808 in the housing walls + 2 × 6708 in the cassette plates), forearm clamped on both
    ends. Loop 40 (R14): the 40T pulley is separate (elbow_pulley) on a double-D, so the tube slides in from one side
    through all four bearings and the pulley."""
    from .belt_stage import dd_flats
    L = HOUSING["y"] + 24                                     # loop 23: +12 (forearm link moved out 6 mm)
    t = Cylinder(20, L, align=MIN) - Cylinder(15, L, align=MIN)
    t -= dd_flats(L / 2 - BELT_Y - 10.0, 20)                  # stage frame: z = L/2 − y (pulley + its flanges)
    return Pos(0, L / 2, 0) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * t


def elbow_pulley() -> Part:
    from .belt_stage import split_pulley
    return Pos(0, BELT_Y, 0) * Rot(-90, 0, 0) * Pos(0, 0, -8) * split_pulley(40, 16, flange=True)


PRINT = {
    "upper_bone": "split at z = -100 (neck/housing) and along the Y centre plane → 3 pieces ≤ 256 mm, bolted + doweled",
    "forearm": "one piece, printed on its side (link plane on the bed), 280 mm diagonal fits the 256 mm bed corner-to-corner",
}


def masses(density=1.27):
    """Printed mass (kg): shells are solid walls; hubs/collars ~70 % effective density (4 perimeters + 25 % infill)."""
    from .belt_stage import small_hub
    from .kx import retainer
    shells = {"upper bone shell": upper_bone().volume, "forearm shell": forearm().volume}
    cf = {"CF tubes (neck Ø40 + forearm Ø30)": (cf_neck().volume + cf_fore().volume) / 1e6 * 1.55}
    kxv = lambda k: kx_hub(k).volume + kx_collar(k).volume + retainer(k).volume
    sockets = {"KX74 sockets ×2 (elbow, yaw: GIM since loop 5)": 2 * kxv(KX74), "KX56 socket (wrist)": kxv(KX56),
               "elbow shaft + split 40T pulley": elbow_pulley_hub().volume + elbow_pulley().volume, "elbow small-pulley hub": small_hub().volume - kx_hub(KX74).volume}
    out = {k: v / 1e6 * density for k, v in shells.items()}
    out.update({k: v / 1e6 * density * 0.7 for k, v in sockets.items()})
    out.update(cf)
    out["bearings: 2×61808 + 2×61804 + 4×61805 (ESTIMATE)"] = 0.020 * 2 + 0.008 * 2 + 0.012 * 4
    out["belt, pins, balls, screws (ESTIMATE)"] = 0.06
    return out


def assembly(elbow_deg: float = -55.0, wrist_deg: float = -35.0):
    """(name, part, colour) for renders. Upper-arm frame; elbow bends about Y (negative = forward, as in the twin)."""
    from .belt_stage import small_hub, belt_loop, pulley
    from .cartridge_s import sleeve, servo_dummy
    from .kx import collar, balls_mated
    out = [("upper_bone", upper_bone(), "white"), ("bone_cap", bone_cap(), "white"), ("e_cassette", cassette(), "dark")]
    # elbow stage, built in the stage frame (cartridge +Z, big pulley at +X), then mapped: +Z→-Y, +X→-Z
    st = Pos(0, -20, -ELBOW_Z + BELT_C) * Rot(90, 0, 0) * Rot(0, 0, -90)
    zc = KX74.gap + KX74.face_t + cm.NECK + cm.FLOOR + cm.MOTOR["h"]
    from .belt_stage import small_stage_v2, V2
    clk = Rot(0, 0, 180)                                     # loop 23: torque pin and cable window point up (elbow_torque_arm)
    out += [("e_can", st * Pos(0, 0, zc) * Rot(180, 0, 0) * clk * cm.can(), "white"),
            ("e_motor", st * Pos(0, 0, zc) * Rot(180, 0, 0) * clk * cm.motor_dummy(), "dark"),
            ("e_collar", st * collar(KX74), "orange")]
    out += [(f"e_{n}", st * pp, c) for n, pp, c in small_stage_v2(20)]                 # loop 38: D13 stage v2 (e_kx_hub_v2, e_shaft_12, …)
    bc = V2["belt_c"]
    out += [("e_belt", st * Pos(0, 0, bc - 7.5) * belt_loop(31.8, 63.7, BELT_C, width=15.0), "dark"),
            ("e_bigpulley", Pos(0, 0, -ELBOW_Z) * elbow_pulley(), "orange"),            # loop 40: split 40T on the elbow shaft's double-D
            ("e_shaft40", Pos(0, 0, -ELBOW_Z) * elbow_pulley_hub(), "steel")]
    # (the yaw stage above the flange lives in rnd/parts/roll.py)
    fe = Pos(0, 0, -ELBOW_Z) * Rot(0, elbow_deg, 0)
    out += [("forearm", fe * forearm(), "white"), ("cf_fore", fe * cf_fore(), "dark"), ("cf_neck", cf_neck(), "dark")]
    # wrist cartridge (DM-J4310 + KX56) + hand (rnd/parts/hand.py), hand centre plane on the arm's centre plane (y = 0)
    from . import hand as hd
    from .kx import KX56
    wr = fe * Pos(0, 0, -FORE_L)
    cart = wr * Pos(0, hd.WRIST_HUB_Y + KX56.gap + hd.cartridge_d_stack(), 0) * Rot(90, 0, 0)
    out += [("wrist_can", cart * hd.can_d(), "white"),
            ("wrist_motor", cart * Pos(0, 0, hd.FLOOR) * Cylinder(J4310["od"] / 2, J4310["h"], align=MIN), "dark"),
            ("wrist_adapter", cart * Pos(0, 0, hd.FLOOR + J4310["h"]) * hd.adapter_d(), "dark")]
    hand = wr * Rot(0, wrist_deg, 0)
    out += [(f"hand_{n}", hand * p, c) for n, p, c in hd.assembly()]
    return out


def cs_shift():
    from .cartridge_s import SERVO
    return SERVO["l"] / 2 - SERVO["shaft_off"]

```

=== FILE: rnd/parts/base.py ===
```python
"""Base v0: 4 omni wheel modules ("+" layout, one wheel forward, axles radial), deck, skirt, battery bay, mast coupling, lidar.

Wheel module (same pattern as every joint): GIM cartridge (torque only) → KX74 wheel hub on a Ø40 shaft in 2 × 61808 in
the pod plates → wheel flange (6 × #10 on a 1.875" circle, AndyMark DuraOmni) → 6" omni wheel. Wheel loads never reach
the motor bearing. The cartridge points inward; the four cartridges leave a centre bay for the 6S battery (slides out
from the back). Deck and skirt are printed in quarters (each ≤ 256 mm).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import math

from build123d import Align, Axis, Box, Cylinder, Part, Pos, Rot, fillet

from ..params import BASELINE
from . import cartridge_m as cm
from .kx import KX74, collar, hub_pip as hub

MIN = (Align.CENTER, Align.CENTER, Align.MIN)
D = BASELINE
R_W = D.base.wheel_radius_pos * 1000          # 210
WHEEL_D, WHEEL_W = D.base.wheel_d * 1000, 46.0
Z_AX = WHEEL_D / 2                            # axle height 76 mm
R_SHELL = D.base.radius * 1000
Z0, Z1 = D.base.clearance * 1000, (D.base.clearance + D.base.height) * 1000
HUB_R = R_W - WHEEL_W / 2 - 48.0              # KX face radius (wheel inner face − hub/bearing stack) = 139
DECK_T = 6.0


def _T(ang):
    """Stage frame (KX face z = 0, cartridge +Z) → world: cartridge points radially inward at wheel angle `ang`."""
    return Rot(0, 0, ang) * Pos(HUB_R, 0, Z_AX) * Rot(0, -90, 0)


def omni_wheel() -> Part:
    """6" DuraOmni placeholder in the stage frame: wheel spans z = -48 … -94 (axis Z)."""
    w = Pos(0, 0, -48 - WHEEL_W) * (Cylinder(60, WHEEL_W, align=MIN) - Cylinder(15, WHEEL_W, align=MIN))
    for row, dz in ((0, -60), (1, -82)):
        for k in range(10):
            a = 2 * math.pi * (k + 0.5 * row) / 10
            w += Pos(66 * math.cos(a), 66 * math.sin(a), dz) * Rot(0, 0, math.degrees(a) + 90) * Rot(0, 90, 0) * Cylinder(10, 26)
    return w


def wheel_shaft() -> Part:
    hb = -hub(KX74).bounding_box().min.Z                                                       # loop 40: follows the D15 socket depth (13.0)
    s = Pos(0, 0, -44) * (Cylinder(20, 44 - hb, align=MIN) - Cylinder(12, 44 - hb, align=MIN))   # Ø40 through the bearings (below the hub)
    s += Pos(0, 0, -48) * (Cylinder(30, 4, align=MIN) - Cylinder(12, 4, align=MIN))            # wheel flange
    return s


def pod() -> Part:
    """Two bearing plates (61808 seats) + top bridge, in the stage frame (stage +x = world +z)."""
    zlo, zhi = Z0 - Z_AX, (Z1 - DECK_T) - Z_AX          # plates from the ground-clearance line to the deck underside
    p = None
    for z0 in (-22.0, -40.0):
        pl = Pos((zlo + zhi) / 2, 0, z0) * Box(zhi - zlo, 80, 8, align=MIN)
        pl -= Pos(0, 0, z0) * Cylinder(26, 8, align=MIN)
        pl -= Pos(-40, 0, z0) * Box(22, 50, 8, align=MIN)                  # lightening below the seat
        p = pl if p is None else p + pl
    bridge = Pos(zhi - 4, 0, -40) * Box(8, 80, 26, align=MIN)                # ties the plates under the deck
    return p + bridge


def wheel_module(ang):
    T = _T(ang)
    zc = KX74.gap + KX74.face_t + cm.NECK + cm.FLOOR + cm.MOTOR["h"]
    return [(f"w{ang}_can", T * Pos(0, 0, zc) * Rot(180, 0, 0) * cm.can(), "white"),
            (f"w{ang}_motor", T * Pos(0, 0, zc) * Rot(180, 0, 0) * cm.motor_dummy(), "dark"),
            (f"w{ang}_hub", T * hub(KX74), "dark"),            # D17 (loop 40, A11): bolted to the GIM output (4 × M4), no KX collar; same envelope
            (f"w{ang}_shaft", T * wheel_shaft(), "orange"), (f"w{ang}_pod", T * pod(), "white"),
            (f"w{ang}_wheel", T * omni_wheel(), "dark"),
            (f"w{ang}_torque_hanger", T * torque_hanger_pose(zc), "white")]


def torque_hanger_pose(zc):
    """Wheel torque hanger in the stage frame: pin at (x 30, y 0) behind the can floor (z = zc … zc + 10); the flange screws
    under the deck (stage x = deck underside); the fork is open downward (stage −x), so the battery top stays clear."""
    tp = cm.TORQUE_PIN
    reach = (Z1 - DECK_T) - Z_AX - tp["r"]
    return Pos(tp["r"], 0, zc) * cm.torque_hanger(reach, slot_in=4.0)


def deck_quarter(q) -> Part:
    """Quarter deck (printed, ribbed), with cut-outs for the wheels."""
    r = R_SHELL - 4
    p = Pos(0, 0, Z1 - DECK_T) * Cylinder(r, DECK_T, align=MIN)
    p &= Rot(0, 0, 90 * q + 45) * Pos(r / 2, r / 2, Z1 - DECK_T) * Box(r, r, DECK_T, align=MIN)      # one diagonal quadrant
    for k in range(4):                                                               # wheel cut-outs (the wheels rise to z 152)
        p -= Rot(0, 0, 90 * k) * Pos(R_W, 0, Z1 - DECK_T) * Box(130, 60, DECK_T, align=MIN)
    p -= Pos(-60 + 40, 0, Z1 - 20) * Box(22, 42, 40, align=MIN)                      # blind-mate plug pass-through
    return p


def rim() -> Part:
    """Looks pass v2: rounded rim ring on the deck edge (printed in 4 arcs with the skirt quarters)."""
    from build123d import fillet as _f
    r = Pos(0, 0, Z1) * (Cylinder(R_SHELL + 2, 8, align=MIN) - Cylinder(R_SHELL - 14, 8, align=MIN))
    try:
        r = _f(r.edges().group_by(Axis.Z)[-1], 3.8)
    except ValueError:
        pass
    for k in range(4):
        r -= Rot(0, 0, 90 * k) * Pos(R_W, 0, Z1 - 5) * Box(130, 60, 20, align=MIN)     # wheel cut-outs
    return r


def skirt_quarter(q) -> Part:
    s = Pos(0, 0, Z0) * (Cylinder(R_SHELL, Z1 - Z0 - DECK_T, align=MIN) - Cylinder(R_SHELL - 2, Z1 - Z0 - DECK_T, align=MIN))
    # loop 27: seams moved into the wheel windows (quarter q spans 90q … 90q + 90°), freeing the 45° azimuth for the dock pads
    s &= Rot(0, 0, 90 * q) * Pos(R_SHELL / 2 + 1, R_SHELL / 2 + 1, Z0) * Box(R_SHELL + 2, R_SHELL + 2, 200, align=MIN)
    for k in range(4):                                                               # wheel windows
        s -= Rot(0, 0, 90 * k) * Pos(R_SHELL, 0, Z0 - 1) * Box(40, 140, 2 * (Z_AX + 62 - Z0) + 2)
    if q == 0:
        s = s + dock_pad_carrier()
        for z, name in DOCK_PADS:
            w, h = (62, 13) if name != "ID" else (42, 11)
            s -= Rot(0, 0, DOCK_PAD_AZ) * Pos(R_SHELL - 0.5, 0, z) * Box(3, w, h)  # pads sit flush in the skirt
    return s


DOCK_PAD_AZ = 45.0              # azimuth of the docking contacts (rnd/dock_sim.py PAD_AZ), between the wheels at 0° and 90°
DOCK_PADS = ((60.0, "+"), (100.0, "−"), (40.0, "ID"))   # pad centre heights above the floor (match rnd/parts/dock.py)


def bumper_light() -> Part:
    """Light ring around the skirt. A12 (loop 40, review finding 13): it stood 4 mm proud at z 43–53 right across the dock
    pads (ID pad at 40, + at 60), so the dock's V faces would have hit the ring, not the pads. The ring now has a 30°
    gap centred on the pads."""
    r = Pos(0, 0, Z0 + 18) * (Cylinder(R_SHELL + 4, 10, align=MIN) - Cylinder(R_SHELL, 10, align=MIN))
    gap = Pos(R_SHELL, 0, Z0 + 13) * Box(2 * R_SHELL * math.sin(math.radians(15)) + 20, 2 * R_SHELL * math.sin(math.radians(15)), 20, align=(Align.CENTER, Align.CENTER, Align.MIN))
    return r - Rot(0, 0, DOCK_PAD_AZ) * gap


def dock_pad_carrier() -> Part:
    """Loop 27: carrier inside the skirt at 45° for the docking contacts. Two brass pads 60 × 12 mm (+ at 60 mm, − at
    100 mm) and a 40 × 10 mm ID pad (40 mm) sit in 1.5 mm pockets, flush with the skirt; wires leave through Ø4 holes.
    Contact force is only the dock's leaf springs (~5 N each), so the 6 mm carrier is ample."""
    c = Pos(R_SHELL - 2 - 3, 0, 28) * Box(6, 84, 90, align=(Align.CENTER, Align.CENTER, Align.MIN))
    c &= Cylinder(R_SHELL - 1.0, 400)                                                # follow the skirt's inner curvature
    for z, name in DOCK_PADS:
        w, h = (62, 13) if name != "ID" else (42, 11)
        c -= Pos(R_SHELL - 1.5, 0, z) * Box(4, w, h)                               # pad pocket through the skirt wall
        c -= Pos(R_SHELL - 5, 0, z) * Rot(0, 90, 0) * Cylinder(2, 12)              # wire hole
    return Rot(0, 0, DOCK_PAD_AZ) * c


def battery():
    return Pos(0, 0, Z0 + 8) * Box(150, 75, 65, align=MIN)          # 6S 10 Ah pack envelope (ESTIMATE)


def coupling():
    """Mast socket on the deck at x = -60: printed block + cam lever (placeholder) + blind-mate plug housing."""
    c = Pos(-60, 0, Z1) * (Box(100, 100, 40, align=MIN) - Box(60.6, 60.6, 40, align=MIN))
    lever = Pos(-60, 58, Z1 + 20) * Box(70, 10, 12)
    plug = Pos(-60 + 40, 0, Z1 - 20) * Box(20, 40, 20, align=MIN)
    return c, lever, plug


def module():
    out = []
    for ang in (0, 90, 180, 270):
        out += wheel_module(ang)
    for q in range(4):
        out += [(f"deck{q}", deck_quarter(q), "white"), (f"skirt{q}", skirt_quarter(q), "white")]
    c, lever, plug = coupling()
    out += [("rim", rim(), "white"), ("battery", battery(), "dark"), ("coupling", c, "white"), ("cam_lever", lever, "orange"), ("plug", plug, "dark"),
            ("lidar", Pos(R_SHELL - 70, 0, Z1) * Cylinder(28, 41, align=MIN), "dark"),
            ("bumper_light", bumper_light(), "orange"),
            ("base_electronics", Rot(0, 0, 45) * Pos(135, 0, Z0 + 10) * Box(50, 70, 30, align=MIN), "dark")]
    return out


def masses(density=1.27):
    pods = 4 * pod().volume / 1e6 * density * 0.6
    shafts = 4 * wheel_shaft().volume / 1e6 * density * 0.7
    hubs = 4 * hub(KX74).volume / 1e6 * density * 0.7                          # D17: no collars on the wheels
    deck = sum(deck_quarter(q).volume for q in range(4)) / 1e6 * density * 0.6
    skirt = sum(skirt_quarter(q).volume for q in range(4)) / 1e6 * density
    c, lever, plug = coupling()
    coup = (c.volume + lever.volume) / 1e6 * density * 0.6
    return {"pods": pods, "wheel shafts + flanges": shafts, "KX74 wheel hubs + collars": hubs, "deck quarters (ribbed)": deck,
            "skirt quarters": skirt, "mast coupling + lever": coup,
            "bearings 8×61808 (ESTIMATE)": 0.16, "cans for 4 wheel cartridges (printed)": 4 * cm.can().volume / 1e6 * density * 0.7,
            "screws, inserts, cam, connector (ESTIMATE)": 0.12}

```

=== FILE: rnd/parts/belt_stage.py ===
```python
"""Printed HTD 5M pulleys and the small-pulley hub (KX74 socket + straddle-mounted pulley on 2 bearings).

Load path: the cartridge (torque only) locks into the hub's KX74 face; belt pull goes into two 61804 bearings
(20×32×7) that sit in the bone walls on both sides of the pulley. Tooth form is an approximation of HTD 5M
(round groove, depth 2.06 mm, pitch-line offset 0.57 mm): print a test pulley and check it against a real belt.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import math

from build123d import Align, Box, Cylinder, Part, Pos, Rot
```
